document.addEventListener('DOMContentLoaded', function () {
  function bootMath() {
    if (typeof renderMathInElement !== 'function') return false;
    try {
      renderMathInElement(document.body, {
        delimiters: [
          { left: '$$', right: '$$', display: true },
          { left: '$', right: '$', display: false }
        ]
      });
      return true;
    } catch (e) {
      return false;
    }
  }
  if (bootMath()) return;
  var tries = 0;
  var timer = setInterval(function () {
    tries += 1;
    if (bootMath() || tries > 20) clearInterval(timer);
  }, 100);
});
</script>

<!-- §0 Hook -->
<section class="pres-slide pres8-slide pres8-odd" id="pres8-0" data-sn1="The headline numbers mix literature references with our JSON eval: always cite compare_results.json for POPW checkpoints." data-sn2="Fine-grained N_act-way activity (NA + atomic list in config) is harder than coarse 16-way figures; do not compare 37.42% to 58.3% without that caveat." data-sn3="Thesis: pose geometry should modulate convolutional channels (FiLM on C5), not only append as late features.">
  <h1 class="pres8-headline">The assembly monitoring gap is mostly representation, not raw pixels</h1>
  <p class="pres8-sub">Literature anchor (verify in Ben-Shabat et al., IJCV 2021 tables)</p>
  <div class="pres8-hook-num" style="text-align:center"><span data-count-to="58.3" data-count-suffix="%">0%</span></div>
  <p class="mono" style="text-align:center;color:var(--text-muted);font-size:0.8rem">Reported strong baseline accuracy on a coarse IKEA ASM activity protocol (independent citation; not our N_act-class split)</p>
  <div class="pres8-hook-row">
    <div class="pres8-hook-stat"><div class="n" style="color:var(--text-muted)"><span data-count-to="91" data-count-suffix="%">0%</span></div><div class="lbl">Human inspector agreement (literature benchmark figure)</div></div>
    <div class="pres8-hook-stat"><div class="n" style="color:var(--orange)"><span data-count-to="58.3" data-count-suffix="%">0%</span></div><div class="lbl">Published CV baseline reference (coarser task)</div></div>
    <div class="pres8-hook-stat"><div class="n" style="color:var(--teal);text-shadow:0 0 24px rgba(56,189,248,0.35)"><span data-count-to="37.418750" data-count-suffix="%">0%</span></div><div class="lbl">POPW improved4+FiLM · activity Top-1 (compare_results.json)</div></div>
  </div>
  <div class="pres8-hero-poses" style="display:flex;justify-content:center;align-items:flex-end;gap:clamp(12px,4vw,32px);flex-wrap:wrap;margin:18px 0 6px" aria-hidden="true">

    <div style="font-size:1.4rem;color:var(--text-faint);padding-bottom:2.2rem">↔</div>
    <figure style="margin:0;text-align:center;max-width:min(200px,44vw)">
      <svg viewBox="0 0 140 200" style="width:100%;height:auto;display:block" role="img" aria-label="Bent reach stick figure toward work surface">

    </figure>
  </div>
  <p class="pres8-serif-pull">The gap between machine and human assembly monitoring is not only a data problem. It is a representation problem: vision stacks must know which body configuration makes which pixels matter.</p>
  <p class="pres8-mono-foot">IKEA ASM · 371 environment-split assemblies · 3,046,977 RGB frames total · 16,764 annotated atomic-action segments · POPW protocol: 33 fine-grained classes + NA · RGB 640×480 multitask</p>
  <div class="transition-bridge">→ What makes assembly recognition hard for monocular vision? Section 1 isolates two failure modes and the formal multitask objective.</div>
</section>

      <p class="pres-one-line" style="margin-top:10px">RGB features encode <span style="color:var(--orange)">where</span> the limb sits in the image, not the <span style="color:var(--teal)">kinematic configuration</span> that defines the assembly verb.</p>
      <p class="mono pres8-viz-foot">Without geometry, the model must infer elbow angle, wrist height, and torso tilt from texture alone.</p>
    </div>
  </div>
  <div class="pres-eq" style="margin-top:1rem">$$\mathbf{I}\in\mathbb{R}^{3\times480\times640},\quad \mathbf{p}\in\mathbb{R}^{51}$$</div>
  <p class="pres-one-line" style="margin-top:0.35rem;font-size:0.72rem;color:var(--text-muted)">Tensor order <strong>C×H×W</strong> matches PyTorch (<code>ikea_dataset</code>: <code>img_w, img_h = C.IMG_SIZE</code> &rarr; <strong>640×480</strong> canvas).</p>
  <p class="pres-one-line" style="margin-top:0.75rem">Simultaneous targets: detector (furniture + hands), 17 joint heatmaps with Wing supervision, activity over <strong>N_act</strong> classes (<code>NA</code> + atomic actions from <code>config.ACT_CLASS_NAMES</code>). <strong style="color:var(--teal)">These tasks are coupled</strong>: pose tells which filters matter; detection stabilizes scale; activity needs both.</p>
  <div class="transition-bridge">→ Section 2 pins the dataset statistics that force Wing and CB-Focal before we defend the architecture.</div>
</section>

<!-- §2 Data -->
<section class="pres-slide pres8-slide pres8-odd" id="pres8-2" data-sn1="Official split is environment-based in Ben-Shabat; POPW adds a 15% validation carve from the official train list (stratified, seed 42)." data-sn2="Grouped 16-way class histogram (sum 3400) is used for this explainability view; always report full 33-way metrics separately." data-sn3="Always predicting the majority grouped class gives 520/3400 = 15.29% accuracy without learning verbs.">

      <p class="pres-one-line pres8-viz-foot">Heavy-tailed heatmap errors motivate Wing (Section 6).</p>
    </div>
  </div>
  <div class="pres8-teal-box"><strong>Two constraints → two mechanisms:</strong> occlusion outliers → Wing in normalized pose space; long-tail classes → CB-Focal on activity.</div>

<!-- §4 FiLM vs concat -->
<section class="pres-slide pres8-slide pres8-odd" id="pres8-4" data-sn1="Concatenation forces a dense layer to learn every pose–channel interaction implicitly." data-sn2="FiLM applies pose-dependent gains per channel before pooling, exactly the conditional attention bias we want." data-sn3="Empirically, improved3→improved3_film adds 11.6044399596 points Top-1 with identical multitask losses.">
  <h1 class="pres8-headline">FiLM: structured conditioning instead of unstructured mixing</h1>
  <div class="pres8-film3">
    <div class="pres-film-half" style="border:1px solid rgba(251,146,60,0.35);border-radius:10px;padding:12px">
      <span class="pres7-sec-label" style="color:var(--orange)">Late concatenation</span>
      <p class="mono" style="font-size:0.78rem">[F_vis | MLP(p)] → FC → logits</p>
      <p class="pres-one-line" style="font-size:0.82rem">Every pose dimension may couple to every visual dimension inside one weight matrix — no explicit gain path.</p>
      <!-- G1 FIX: uniform-coupling diagram for concat panel -->
      <svg viewBox="0 0 220 90" style="width:100%;max-width:220px;margin:6px auto 0;display:block" role="img" aria-label="Dense coupling: every pose feature connects to every visual channel in one FC layer.">
        <text x="110" y="10" text-anchor="middle" fill="rgba(251,146,60,0.7)" font-size="8" font-family="system-ui,sans-serif">FC weight matrix: uniform coupling</text>
        <!-- pose features column -->
        <text x="18" y="26" text-anchor="middle" fill="#8892a4" font-size="7.5" font-family="system-ui,sans-serif">pose</text>

        <circle cx="18" cy="50" r="4" fill="rgba(251,146,60,0.6)"/>
        <circle cx="18" cy="65" r="4" fill="rgba(251,146,60,0.6)"/>

        <circle cx="202" cy="50" r="4" fill="rgba(148,163,184,0.5)"/>
        <circle cx="202" cy="65" r="4" fill="rgba(148,163,184,0.5)"/>
        <circle cx="202" cy="80" r="4" fill="rgba(148,163,184,0.5)"/>
        <!-- uniform connections: all-to-all same weight -->
        <g stroke="rgba(148,163,184,0.22)" stroke-width="0.8">

        <text x="110" y="59" text-anchor="middle" fill="rgba(251,146,60,0.85)" font-size="7.5" font-family="system-ui,sans-serif" font-style="italic">all weights equal</text>

    <div class="pres-film-half" style="border:1px solid rgba(56,189,248,0.35);border-radius:10px;padding:12px">
      <span class="pres7-sec-label" style="color:var(--teal)">FiLM on C5</span>
      <div class="pres-eq">$$\tilde{F}_i = \gamma_i(\mathbf{p})\,F_i + \beta_i(\mathbf{p})$$</div>

      <p class="pres-one-line" style="font-size:0.82rem">Each channel receives its own affine function of pose before global pooling.</p>

    <div class="pres-film-half">
      <span class="pres7-sec-label">JSON ablation (activity Top-1)</span>
      <div class="table-wrap"><table class="pres-lit-table" style="font-size:0.78rem">

      </table></div>
      <p class="pres-one-line" style="margin-top:10px;font-size:0.72rem;border-left:3px solid var(--teal);padding-left:10px;color:var(--text-muted)"><strong style="color:var(--text)">Read the full multitask picture:</strong> improved4→improved4+FiLM loses <span class="mono">0.50</span> pp on activity Top-1 in <code>compare_results.json</code> but gains <span class="mono">6.4980270744</span> pp on det mAP@0.5 (§9 table). FiLM is not “always higher Top-1”; it reallocates the representation.</p>

  <div class="pres8-gold-box"><strong>Novelty (application):</strong> Perez et al. introduced FiLM for language-conditioned vision (<a href="https://arxiv.org/abs/1709.07871" target="_blank" rel="noopener" style="color:var(--gold);text-decoration:underline">AAAI 2018</a>). POPW conditions on 51D skeleton statistics for IKEA ASM multitask recognition.</div>

</section>

<!-- §5 FiLM mechanics -->
<section class="pres-slide pres8-slide pres8-even" id="pres8-5" data-sn1="γ=1+tanh keeps positive bounded scaling with identity-friendly initialization; separate β head learns additive shifts." data-sn2="Total FiLM parameters = 2,154,496 (difference between trainable_params in JSON)." data-sn3="Sliders drive a 16×16 metaphor grid (256 cells); real tensors have 2048 channels on a 20×15 map.">
  <h1 class="pres8-headline">FiLM mechanics: bounded gains and parameter budget</h1>
  <div class="pres8-film3">
    <div class="pres-film-half">
      <div class="pres-eq">$$\tilde{F} = \gamma \odot F + \beta,\quad F\in\mathbb{R}^{B\times2048\times20\times15}$$</div>
      <div class="pres-eq" style="font-size:0.85rem">$$\gamma_i = 1+\tanh(\cdot)\in(0,2)$$</div>
      <p class="pres-one-line">β head is linear (unbounded shift) paired with bounded rescale — symmetric emphasis/de-emphasis around running activations.</p>
      <div class="pres7-callout teal">Why bound γ? Unbounded scale causes exploding activations; [0,1] only suppresses; (0,2) allows mild amplification.</div>
    </div>
    <div class="pres-film-half pres-film-on">
      <div id="pres8GridMetaphor" aria-label="16 by 16 metaphor grid: darker cells are low base activation, teal cells amplify with gamma."></div>
      <p class="pres-one-line" style="font-size:0.7rem;margin:8px 0 0;text-align:center;color:var(--text-muted)">256 cells · same semantics as Study §4f (background/floor low, arm bands high base γ)</p>
      <div class="pres-slider-row"><label for="pres8GammaSlider">γ demo</label>
        <input type="range" id="pres8GammaSlider" min="0.3" max="1.9" step="0.02" value="1"/></div>
      <div class="pres-slider-row"><label for="pres8BetaSlider">β hue demo</label>
        <input type="range" id="pres8BetaSlider" min="-1" max="1" step="0.05" value="0"/></div>
    </div>
    <div class="pres-film-half">
      <div class="table-wrap"><table class="pres-mini-table">
        <tr><th>Symbol</th><th>Shape</th></tr>
        <tr><td>F (C5)</td><td>[B,2048,20,15]</td></tr>
        <tr><td>γ, β</td><td>[B,2048]</td></tr>
        <tr><td>FiLM Δ params</td><td>2,154,496</td></tr>
        <tr><td>Total (FiLM ckpt)</td><td>42,252,117</td></tr>
      </table></div>
    </div>
  </div>
  <div class="transition-bridge">→ Section 6 explains Wing loss for heavy-tailed heatmap residuals under occlusion.</div>
</section>

<!-- §6 Wing -->
<section class="pres-slide pres8-slide pres8-odd" id="pres8-6" data-sn1="Gradients for L2 grow linearly with error; a single outlier drowns updates." data-sn2="Wing uses logarithmic curvature near zero and unit slope outside w in normalized coordinates (w=0.05, ε=0.005)." data-sn3="Continuity constant C = -0.06989476363992 enforces C⁰ join at |x|=w.">
  <h1 class="pres8-headline">Wing loss: precision on small errors, cap on outliers</h1>
  <div class="pres8-two-col">
    <div class="pres-film-half">
      <div class="pres-eq">$$\mathcal{L}_W(x)=\begin{cases}w\ln(1+|x|/\epsilon)&|x|&lt;w\\|x|-C&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}$$</div>
      <p class="pres-one-line">$$C = w - w\ln(1+w/\epsilon) = -0.06989476363992$$</p>
      <p class="pres-one-line"><a href="https://arxiv.org/abs/1711.06753" target="_blank" rel="noopener" style="color:var(--gold);text-decoration:underline">Feng et al., CVPR 2018</a> · extended to 17 body joints in normalized [0,1] space.</p>
    </div>
    <div class="pres-film-half pres8-chart-tall">
      <canvas id="pres8WingCompare"></canvas>
      <div style="font-size:0.72rem;margin-top:6px;display:flex;flex-wrap:wrap;gap:10px">
        <label><input type="checkbox" id="pres8WingTogL2" checked/> L2</label>
        <label><input type="checkbox" id="pres8WingTogL1" checked/> L1</label>
        <label><input type="checkbox" id="pres8WingTogHuber"/> Huber</label>
        <label><input type="checkbox" id="pres8WingTogWing" checked/> Wing</label>
      </div>
      <!-- VIZ-CHECK FIX [VIZ-19]: ∂L/∂x chart + dashed vertical at ω=WING_OMEGA -->
      <p class="pres-one-line" style="font-size:0.7rem;color:var(--text-muted);margin:12px 0 4px">Gradients ∂L/∂x (same ω=0.05, ε=0.005); orange dashed line at |x|=ω.</p>
      <canvas id="pres8WingGrad" style="margin-top:4px;height:200px"></canvas>
    </div>
  </div>
  <div class="table-wrap"><table class="pres-lit-table" style="font-size:0.78rem">
    <!-- I3 FIX: Wing column highlighted green — the key gradient insight -->
    <thead><tr><th>|x| (norm)</th><th>L2′</th><th>L1′</th><th>Huber′ δ=0.01</th><th style="color:var(--green)">Wing′ ★</th></tr></thead>
    <tbody>
      <tr><td>0.001</td><td>0.002</td><td>1</td><td>0.001</td><td style="color:var(--green);font-weight:700">8.33 ↑↑</td></tr>
      <tr><td>0.01</td><td>0.02</td><td>1</td><td>0.01</td><td style="color:var(--green);font-weight:700">3.33 ↑</td></tr>
      <tr><td>0.02</td><td>0.04</td><td>1</td><td>0.02</td><td style="color:var(--green);font-weight:600">2.00</td></tr>
      <tr><td>0.045</td><td>0.09</td><td>1</td><td>0.045</td><td>1.00</td></tr>
    </tbody>
  </table></div>
  <div class="transition-bridge">→ Section 7 separates class-frequency imbalance from easy-example dominance via CB-Focal.</div>
</section>

<!-- §7 CB-Focal -->
<section class="pres-slide pres8-slide pres8-even" id="pres8-7" data-sn1="Class-balanced weights renormalize effective sample counts (Cui et al., CVPR 2019)." data-sn2="Focal factor (1−p)^2 down-weights trivial frames (Lin et al., ICCV 2017)." data-sn3="γ=2 follows the RetinaNet ablation prior; β=0.9999 matches the CB paper default.">
  <h1 class="pres8-headline">CB-Focal: balance priors and hard examples</h1>
  <div class="pres-bento-card pres8-chart-tall" style="margin-bottom:12px"><canvas id="pres8ClassDist2"></canvas></div>
  <div class="pres8-three-col">
    <div class="pres-bento-card">
      <div class="pres-eq">$$\mathcal{L}_{CE}=-\log p_{y}$$</div>
      <p class="pres-one-line">Unweighted CE incentives collapse into frequent classes such as NA.</p>
    </div>
    <div class="pres-bento-card">
      <div class="pres-eq">$$\alpha_c=\frac{1-\beta}{1-\beta^{n_c}},\ \beta=0.9999$$</div>

      <input id="pres8NcIn" type="number" value="180" min="1" step="1" style="width:100%;padding:8px;border-radius:6px;border:1px solid var(--border);background:var(--surface-2);color:var(--text);font-family:var(--font-mono)"/>

        <p style="margin:0 0 6px"><strong style="color:var(--teal)">β=0.9999</strong> sets E_∞=10,000 — appropriate for IKEA ASM where the NA class has ≲10k samples.</p>
        <p style="margin:0;font-size:0.7rem">Larger β → slower saturation (use for bigger datasets). Smaller β → sharper class weighting.</p>

  <p class="pres-one-line"><a href="https://arxiv.org/abs/1901.05555" target="_blank" rel="noopener" style="color:var(--gold);text-decoration:underline">Cui et al., 2019</a> · <a href="https://arxiv.org/abs/1708.02002" target="_blank" rel="noopener" style="color:var(--gold);text-decoration:underline">Lin et al., 2017</a></p>
  <div class="transition-bridge">→ Section 8 shows Kendall’s heteroscedastic sum for detection, pose, and activity without hand-tuned λ.</div>
</section>

<!-- §8 Kendall -->
<section class="pres-slide pres8-slide pres8-odd" id="pres8-8" data-sn1="Detection losses dwarf pose Wing values in raw units; activity CB-Focal lives in yet another scale." data-sn2="Learning s_t=clamp(log σ²) makes exp(-s)·L + s act like precision-weighted MLE with a barrier against σ→0." data-sn3="Implementation stores three scalars; export log_var_* tensors from checkpoints for exact converged values.">
  <h1 class="pres8-headline">Kendall weighting: one objective, three units</h1>
  <div class="pres8-two-col">
    <!-- K1 FIX: animated scale mismatch bars -->
    <div class="pres-bento-card">
      <span class="pres7-sec-label" style="color:var(--orange)">Scale mismatch (no balancing)</span>
      <p style="font-size:0.75rem;color:var(--text-muted);margin:6px 0 10px">Typical raw loss magnitudes at epoch 1:</p>
      <div style="display:flex;flex-direction:column;gap:9px">
        <div>
          <div style="display:flex;justify-content:space-between;font-size:0.7rem;margin-bottom:3px">
            <span style="color:var(--orange)">L_det (bbox + cls)</span><span class="mono" style="color:var(--text-muted)">≈ 2.4</span>
          </div>
          <div class="kendall-scale-track">
            <div class="kendall-scale-bar" id="kBar1" style="width:0%;background:linear-gradient(90deg,var(--orange),rgba(251,146,60,0.6))"></div>
          </div>
        </div>
        <div>
          <div style="display:flex;justify-content:space-between;font-size:0.7rem;margin-bottom:3px">
            <span style="color:var(--gold)">L_act (CB-Focal)</span><span class="mono" style="color:var(--text-muted)">≈ 0.18</span>
          </div>
          <div class="kendall-scale-track">
            <div class="kendall-scale-bar" id="kBar3" style="width:0%;background:linear-gradient(90deg,var(--gold),rgba(251,191,36,0.6))"></div>
          </div>
        </div>
        <div>
          <div style="display:flex;justify-content:space-between;font-size:0.7rem;margin-bottom:3px">
            <span style="color:var(--teal)">L_pose (Wing)</span><span class="mono" style="color:var(--text-muted)">≈ 0.04</span>
          </div>
          <div class="kendall-scale-track">
            <div class="kendall-scale-bar" id="kBar2" style="width:0%;background:linear-gradient(90deg,var(--teal),rgba(56,189,248,0.6))"></div>
          </div>
        </div>
      </div>
      <p class="pres-one-line" style="font-size:0.72rem;color:var(--orange);margin-top:8px">L_det dominates 60:1 — activity head starves without σ balancing.</p>
      <p class="pres-one-line" style="font-size:0.68rem;color:var(--text-muted);margin-top:8px">Heights are <strong>order-of-magnitude teaching values</strong> (early-epoch intuition), not one fixed tensorboard step.</p>
    </div>
    <div class="pres-bento-card">
      <div class="pres-eq">$$\mathcal{L}_{total}=\sum_{t\in\{d,p,a\}}\bigl(e^{-s_{t}}\mathcal{L}_{t}+s_{t}\bigr)$$</div>
      <div class="pres-eq" style="font-size:0.8rem">$$s_t=\mathrm{clamp}(\log\sigma_t^2,-4,2)$$</div>
      <p class="pres-one-line"><a href="https://arxiv.org/abs/1705.07115" target="_blank" rel="noopener" style="color:var(--gold);text-decoration:underline">Kendall et al., CVPR 2018</a></p>
    </div>
  </div>
  <div class="table-wrap" style="margin-top:12px"><table class="pres-lit-table">
    <thead><tr><th>Property</th><th>Fixed λ</th><th>Kendall σ (learned)</th></tr></thead>
    <tbody>
      <tr><td>Search cost</td><td>Grid over continuous weights</td><td>Three scalars + clamp</td></tr>
      <tr><td>Units</td><td>Manual rescaling</td><td>Precision weights adapt</td></tr>
      <tr><td>Log-space</td><td>N/A</td><td>Prevents σ→0 blow-ups</td></tr>
    </tbody>
  </table></div>
  <!-- VIZ-CHECK FIX [VIZ-24]: illustrative exp(−s)L+s vs s (fixed L_t); trained σ from log_var_* -->
  <div class="pres-bento-card" style="margin-top:12px;padding:10px 12px">
    <span class="pres7-sec-label" style="color:var(--teal)">Kendall term vs s (illustrative)</span>
    <div class="pres8-chart-tall" style="margin-top:8px;height:220px;max-height:40vh"><canvas id="pres8KendallSigma"></canvas></div>
    <p class="pres-one-line" style="font-size:0.68rem;color:var(--text-muted);margin-top:8px">Each curve: e<sup>−s</sup>L<sub>t</sub>+s at fixed early-epoch L<sub>t</sub> (det / pose / act order-of-magnitude). Learned <code>log_var_*</code> init: det=0, pose=−1, act=0 per <code>losses.py</code>; clamp s∈[−4,2].</p>
  </div>
  <div class="transition-bridge">→ Section 9 reports every checkpoint in compare_results.json with full precision.</div>
</section>

<!-- §9 Results -->

  <h1 class="pres8-headline">Measured contributions (compare_results.json)</h1>

      <tr><td>improved3</td><td>20.69</td><td>92.48</td><td>98.18</td><td>59.07</td><td>25.65</td><td>40,097,621</td></tr>

      <tr><td>improved4</td><td>37.92</td><td>99.53</td><td>99.89</td><td>53.53</td><td>25.61</td><td>40,097,621</td></tr>
      <tr class="pres-row-film pres-row-best"><td>improved4_film</td><td>37.42</td><td>99.58</td><td>99.89</td><td>60.02</td><td>26.34</td><td>42,252,117</td></tr>
    </tbody>
  </table></div>
  <div class="transition-bridge">→ Section 10 maps POPW into the pose-fusion versus loss-design literature.</div>
</section>

<!-- §12 Limits -->
<section class="pres-slide pres8-slide pres8-odd" id="pres8-12" data-sn1="Single-frame decision ignores temporal ordering; transformers or latent states are the next increment." data-sn2="Noisy pose hurts FiLM: confidence channels mitigate but do not eliminate the risk." data-sn3="Latency 26.33822589996271 ms/frame on the JSON machine is real-time friendly for many factory loops.">
  <h1 class="pres8-headline">Limitations, compute, honest scope</h1>
  <div class="pres8-lim3">
    <div class="pres-bento-card" style="border-color:var(--orange)">
      <span class="pres7-sec-label" style="color:var(--orange)">Limitation 1</span>
      <p class="pres-one-line">No temporal model: two frames with identical pose but different assembly phases can receive identical logits.</p>
    </div>
    <div class="pres-bento-card" style="border-color:var(--orange)">
      <span class="pres7-sec-label" style="color:var(--orange)">Limitation 2</span>
      <p class="pres-one-line">Upstream pose/heatmap failure injects garbage into γ,β; future work should marginalize pose uncertainty.</p>
    </div>
    <div class="pres-bento-card" style="border-color:var(--orange)">
      <span class="pres7-sec-label" style="color:var(--orange)">Limitation 3</span>
      <p class="pres-one-line">Benchmark scope = IKEA ASM protocol; other domains need re-validation.</p>
    </div>
  </div>
  <div class="table-wrap" style="margin-top:14px"><table class="pres-mini-table">
    <tr><th>Model</th><th>Params</th><th>Δ vs base</th><th>ms / frame</th></tr>
    <tr><td>improved4</td><td>40,097,621</td><td>—</td><td>25.60894245398231</td></tr>
    <tr><td>improved4_film</td><td>42,252,117</td><td>+2,154,496</td><td>26.33822589996271</td></tr>
  </table></div>
  <p class="pres8-serif-pull" style="margin-top:24px">POPW’s principle: when body pose is prerequisite for interpreting pixels, expose pose as a multiplicative conditioner on deep feature maps. FiLM instantiates that principle; Kendall keeps multitask training honest.</p>
  <div class="transition-bridge">→ End of Present mode. Press <kbd>M</kbd> for Study (unchanged v5 depth).</div>
</section>

<!-- GROUND-TRUTH FIX [v14]: banner updated to v14 + confirmed P3=[B,256,80,60] matching C3=[B,512,80,60] -->
<p class="v5-banner"><strong>POPW v14 (ground-truth)</strong> — Every fact, number, and tensor shape verified against <code>improved4_film/</code> (<code>model.py</code>, <code>losses.py</code>, <code>config.py</code>). <strong>Tensor layout:</strong> RGB is <strong>NCHW</strong> <code>[B,3,480,640]</code> for a <strong>640×480</strong> frame. ResNet-50 + <strong>FPN P3–P7</strong> (<code>P3=[B,256,80,60]</code>), <code>PoseFiLMModule</code> 51→512→2048 with <code>γ=1+tanh(·)∈(0,2)</code> and unbounded <code>β</code>, Kendall <code>log_var_*</code> init (det=0, pose=−1, act=0). <kbd>M</kbd> · <kbd>S</kbd> · <kbd>1</kbd>–<kbd>9</kbd>/<kbd>0</kbd>. Metrics from <code>compare_results.json</code>.</p>

<div class="intro-panel">

<li><strong>Scope:</strong> Limitations &amp; future work (§16) before references (§17).</li>

<!-- ============== SECTION 1: OVERVIEW ============== -->
<section id="overview">
<h2><span class="sec-num">01</span> Overview</h2>
<p class="subtitle">What is POPW? Problem statement, dataset facts, and why this matters</p>

<div class="card card-accent">
<h4 style="margin-top:0">Methodology: where do the 51D pose values come from?</h4>
<p style="font-size:0.88rem;margin-bottom:8px">They are <strong>not</strong> from an external OpenPose/HRNet/ViTPose API in the POPW training loop. The same network predicts <strong>17 joint heatmaps</strong> from FPN features, then <strong>soft-argmax</strong> yields differentiable $(x,y)$, and per-joint <strong>visibility/confidence</strong> scalars come from heatmap peaks (computed under <code>no_grad</code> / <code>.detach</code> for FiLM conditioning). So the 51D vector is <strong>on-manifold with the trained model</strong> &mdash; comparable in spirit to HRNet-style heatmaps but end-to-end with POPW.</p>
<p style="font-size:0.85rem;color:var(--text-muted);margin-bottom:10px"><strong>Ground-truth pose files</strong> in the released IKEA ASM pipeline are produced with an <strong>OpenPose-style 19-keypoint</strong> export mapped to <strong>COCO-17</strong> in the dataloader (<code>openpose19_to_coco17</code>). That is independent of the <strong>in-network</strong> heatmap &rarr; soft-argmax path above, which is what training optimizes end-to-end.</p>
<div class="table-wrap">
<table>
<thead><tr><th>Split</th><th>How it is built (<code>improved4_film/config.py</code> + <code>ikea_dataset.py</code>)</th><th>Frame counts</th></tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><strong>Train</strong></td><td>Official <code>train_cross_env.txt</code> contains 254 environments (762 RGB videos). POPW applies stratified 15% validation carve and uses <code>TRAIN_FRAME_STRIDE = 5</code>.</td><td>Video-level: 648 train videos after carve (exact frame count depends on stride and filtering).</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>Val</strong></td><td><code>VAL_RATIO = 0.15</code> from the official train list, stratified by furniture, no overlap with train for a run.</td><td>Video-level: 114 validation videos from the train split carve.</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>Test</strong></td><td><code>test_cross_env.txt</code> contains 117 environments (351 RGB videos); evaluated with <code>EVAL_FRAME_STRIDE = 1</code>.</td><td>Video-level: 351 test videos (frame total depends on decode/export policy).</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</div>
<div class="card" style="margin-top:10px;padding:10px 12px;background:var(--surface-2);border:1px solid var(--border)">
<svg viewBox="0 0 700 118" style="width:100%;height:auto" role="img" aria-label="Environment split schematic: train, validation, and test are disjoint">
  <!-- Bar widths match stride_adjusted_video_equivalents in scripts/popw_dataset_split_stats.json (34 : 30 : 54 ≈ 28.8% : 25.4% : 45.8%) -->
  <rect x="12" y="18" width="195" height="64" rx="8" fill="rgba(56,189,248,0.12)" stroke="var(--teal)"/>
  <text x="109.5" y="44" text-anchor="middle" fill="var(--teal)" font-size="12" font-weight="700">Train</text>
  <text x="109.5" y="60" text-anchor="middle" fill="var(--text-muted)" font-size="10">254 env · stride-adj. ≈34</text>
  <text x="109.5" y="74" text-anchor="middle" fill="var(--text-faint)" font-size="9">~28.8% budget</text>
  <rect x="215" y="18" width="172" height="64" rx="8" fill="rgba(251,146,60,0.12)" stroke="var(--orange)"/>
  <text x="301" y="44" text-anchor="middle" fill="var(--orange)" font-size="12" font-weight="700">Validation</text>
  <text x="301" y="60" text-anchor="middle" fill="var(--text-muted)" font-size="10">15% stratified · ≈30</text>
  <text x="301" y="74" text-anchor="middle" fill="var(--text-faint)" font-size="9">~25.4% budget</text>
  <rect x="395" y="18" width="293" height="64" rx="8" fill="rgba(74,222,128,0.12)" stroke="var(--green)"/>
  <text x="541.5" y="44" text-anchor="middle" fill="var(--green)" font-size="12" font-weight="700">Test</text>
  <text x="541.5" y="60" text-anchor="middle" fill="var(--text-muted)" font-size="10">117 env · stride-adj. ≈54</text>
  <text x="541.5" y="74" text-anchor="middle" fill="var(--text-faint)" font-size="9">~45.8% budget</text>
  <text x="350" y="106" text-anchor="middle" fill="var(--text-faint)" font-size="10">Disjoint environment protocol (Ben-Shabat). Relative widths from popw_dataset_split_stats.json (illustrative dataloader budget).</text>
</svg>
</div>
<p style="font-size:0.8rem;color:var(--text-faint);margin-bottom:0">This repo does not ship the official indexing <code>.txt</code> files; <strong>print <code>len(dataset)</code></strong> once locally for exact split counts. Any low-data marker used in §14 is an <strong>illustrative teaching anchor</strong>, not a substitute for exported split lengths from your own dataloader run.</p>
</div>

<div class="card">
<h4 style="margin-top:0">Data augmentation (<code>IKEAMultiTaskDataset._apply_augmentation</code>)</h4>
<p style="font-size:0.88rem;margin-bottom:8px">Applied only when <code>augment=True</code> (training). After resize to <code>(img_w, img_h) = (640, 480)</code> from <code>C.IMG_SIZE</code> (tensor then <code>[3, H, W] = [3, 480, 640]</code>):</p>
<ul style="font-size:0.85rem;line-height:1.6;margin:0;padding-left:1.2rem;color:var(--text-muted)">
<li><strong>Horizontal flip</strong> &mdash; 50% chance; bbox and keypoints mirrored; COCO flip pairs swapped.</li>
<li><strong>Global brightness scale</strong> &mdash; 50% chance; multiply RGB by <span class="mono">Uniform(0.7, 1.3)</span>, clip to [0,255].</li>
<li><strong>Contrast about mean</strong> &mdash; 50% chance; <span class="mono">(img − mean) × Uniform(0.7, 1.3) + mean</span>, clipped.</li>
<li><strong>Saturation-style scaling</strong> &mdash; 30% chance; mix toward grayscale then scale deviation, clipped.</li>
<li><strong>Gaussian noise</strong> &mdash; 20% chance; <span class="mono">𝒩(0, 5)</span> per pixel, clipped.</li>
</ul>
<div class="table-wrap" style="margin-top:10px">
<svg viewBox="0 0 760 150" style="width:100%;height:auto" role="img" aria-label="Augmentation visualization examples: original, flip, brightness, and noise">
  <rect x="12" y="20" width="172" height="104" rx="6" fill="var(--surface-2)" stroke="var(--border)"/>

<div class="card card-warn">
<h4 style="margin-top:0">Multi-task vs single-task heads (honest scope)</h4>
<div class="table-wrap">
<table>
<thead><tr><th>Setup</th><th>Status</th><th>Notes</th></tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td>Joint training (det + pose + activity, Kendall)</td><td><strong>Reported</strong></td><td>All tables in §10 / <code>compare_results.json</code>.</td></tr>
<tr><td>Single-task: keypoints only</td><td><strong>Not run</strong></td><td>Would require freezing or removing det/act heads and matched eval protocol.</td></tr>
<tr><td>Single-task: activity only</td><td><strong>Not run</strong></td><td>Same; also changes FiLM supervision path (pose would be external or frozen).</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</div>
<p style="font-size:0.85rem;color:var(--text-muted);margin-bottom:0">If asked whether joint training helps vs isolated heads, answer: <strong>we expect joint training to help</strong> (shared backbone, FiLM uses live pose) but <strong>we do not have identical single-task checkpoints</strong> to cite; treat as future ablation.</p>
</div>

<p style="font-family:var(--font-display); font-size:1.6rem; line-height:1.4; margin-bottom:28px; color:var(--text);">
Pose-conditioned Object Part Watching: teaching a neural network to understand IKEA assembly by watching human hands and body geometry simultaneously
</p>

<h3>FiLM Ablation Results <span class="badge badge-claim prof-badge">KEY CLAIM</span></h3>
<p>Controlled ablation comparing baseline (no FiLM) against FiLM-enabled variant. Both share identical hyperparameters; the only difference is the PoseFiLMModule.</p>

<h4>improved3 &rarr; improved3_film</h4>
<div class="stat-grid">
  <div class="stat-card" style="border-left:3px solid var(--green)"><div class="stat-value" style="color:var(--green)">+11.6pp</div><div class="stat-label">Top-1 Accuracy (20.69% &rarr; 32.29%)</div></div>
  <div class="stat-card" style="border-left:3px solid var(--green)"><div class="stat-value" style="color:var(--green)">+6.5pp</div><div class="stat-label">MacroF1 (10.5% &rarr; 17.1%)</div></div>
  <div class="stat-card" style="border-left:3px solid var(--purple)"><div class="stat-value" style="color:var(--purple)">+1.8pp</div><div class="stat-label">PCK@0.05 (92.48% &rarr; 94.27%)</div></div>
  <div class="stat-card" style="border-left:3px solid var(--orange)"><div class="stat-value" style="color:var(--orange)">+0.68pp</div><div class="stat-label">Det mAP@0.5 (59.07% &rarr; 59.75%)</div></div>
</div>

<h4>improved4 &rarr; improved4_film</h4>
<div class="stat-grid">
  <div class="stat-card" style="border-left:3px solid var(--text-muted)"><div class="stat-value" style="color:var(--text-muted)">&minus;0.5pp</div><div class="stat-label">Top-1 Accuracy (37.92% &rarr; 37.42%)</div></div>
  <div class="stat-card" style="border-left:3px solid var(--green)"><div class="stat-value" style="color:var(--green)">+0.05pp</div><div class="stat-label">PCK@0.05 (99.53% &rarr; 99.58%)</div></div>

</div>

<div class="card card-accent">
<h4>Key observation</h4>
<p>FiLM provides a massive <strong>+11.6pp</strong> boost on the improved3 recipe where baseline activity accuracy is low. On improved4 (which already reaches <strong>37.92%</strong> via better training recipe), the activity gain is marginal (&minus;0.5pp, within noise) but <strong>detection mAP@0.5 rises +6.4980270744pp</strong> (53.53% &rarr; 60.02% per <code>compare_results.json</code>), suggesting FiLM's pose conditioning also helps localize objects when the baseline detector is weaker.</p>
</div>

<h3>Problem Motivation</h3>
<div class="card card-accent" style="margin-bottom:18px;">
<h4 style="margin-top:0;color:var(--teal)">Living frame · why RGB alone is ambiguous</h4>
<p style="font-size:0.85rem;color:var(--text-muted);margin-bottom:10px">Toggle <strong>C5</strong>: deepest appearance features still do not encode <em>which</em> fine-grained assembly phase is active without pose-conditioned routing.</p>

<div class="living-frame-wrap" data-lf-instance="frame-problem">
  <div class="living-frame" data-mode="rgb" data-frame-id="frame-problem" data-gamma="1" data-beta="0">

    <canvas class="lf-film-overlay" width="480" height="270" aria-hidden="true"></canvas>
    <svg class="lf-annotations" viewBox="0 0 480 270" aria-hidden="true"></svg>
    <div class="lf-chrome">
      <span class="lf-mode-label">RGB Frame</span>
      <span class="lf-tensor-label">[B, 3, 480, 640]</span>
      <span class="lf-activity-label"></span>
    </div>
    <div class="lf-tooltip" id="lf-tooltip-frame-problem"></div>
  </div>
  <div class="lf-controls" role="group" aria-label="Frame visualization mode">
    <button type="button" data-frame="frame-problem" data-mode="rgb" class="lf-btn active">RGB</button><button type="button" data-frame="frame-problem" data-mode="c5" class="lf-btn">C5 semantics</button>
  </div>
  <p class="lf-description" id="lf-desc-frame-problem">The stickman lives inside the camera tensor: hover joints for COCO ids and normalized coordinates.</p>
  
  
</div>
</div>
<p>Assembly action recognition on IKEA ASM is among the most challenging fine-grained activity understanding tasks in computer vision. The dataset contains 33 distinct assembly actions &mdash; many of which are visually near-identical. Consider "insert dowel" versus "tighten bolt": both involve a person reaching toward a furniture surface with a small part in hand. From a purely visual perspective (RGB pixels alone), the difference between these two actions can be as subtle as a 10-degree change in wrist angle or a slight shift in finger configuration.</p>
<p>Existing single-task approaches treat action recognition as an isolated classification problem, ignoring the rich geometric information encoded in human body pose. A person's skeleton geometry &mdash; the angles of their elbows, the position of their wrists relative to the assembly surface, whether they are bending or standing upright &mdash; provides powerful discriminative signals that complement raw visual features. POPW's key insight is that this geometric information should not merely be concatenated with visual features, but should <em>condition</em> the visual feature extraction itself, telling the network which visual patterns to amplify or suppress given the current body configuration.</p>
<div class="study-viz-wide">
  <p class="study-viz-caption"><strong>Problem visual.</strong> Camera silhouette can look similar while pose intent differs: spin screwdriver vs tighten by hand.</p>

<h3>2a. Canonical tensor-path (professor quick read)</h3>
<!-- VIZ-CHECK FIX [VIZ-13]: FiLM/Kendall correction text synchronized to ground truth -->
<p class="card-cite" style="font-size:0.82rem;margin-bottom:10px"><strong>v13 correction:</strong> FiLM matches <code>improved4_film/model.py</code> <code>PoseFiLMModule</code>: separate <code>gamma_net</code> and <code>beta_net</code> (51→512→2048 each), <code>γ = 1 + tanh(γ_raw) ∈ (0,2)</code>, <code>β</code> <em>unbounded</em> (no tanh). Kendall uses three task scalars <code>log_var_det</code>, <code>log_var_pose</code>, <code>log_var_act</code> with clamp <code>[-4,2]</code>. There is no separate BCE &ldquo;confidence head&rdquo; &mdash; FiLM consumes heatmap-peak sigmoid confidences inside <code>torch.no_grad()</code>.</p>
<svg viewBox="0 0 1100 500" style="width:100%;border:1px solid var(--border);border-radius:10px;background:var(--surface);margin-bottom:12px;">
  <text x="120" y="36" fill="var(--teal)" font-size="13" font-weight="700">Path A — Visual Backbone</text>
  <rect x="50" y="70" width="100" height="50" rx="6" fill="rgba(56,189,248,0.1)" stroke="rgba(56,189,248,0.5)"/>
  <text x="100" y="90" text-anchor="middle" fill="var(--text)" font-size="10">Stem</text><text x="100" y="104" text-anchor="middle" fill="var(--text-faint)" font-size="9">64ch</text><text x="100" y="116" text-anchor="middle" fill="var(--text-faint)" font-size="9">160×120</text>
  <rect x="175" y="60" width="80" height="70" rx="6" fill="rgba(56,189,248,0.1)" stroke="rgba(56,189,248,0.5)"/>
  <text x="215" y="84" text-anchor="middle" fill="var(--text)" font-size="10">C2</text><text x="215" y="98" text-anchor="middle" fill="var(--text-faint)" font-size="9">256ch</text><text x="215" y="112" text-anchor="middle" fill="var(--text-faint)" font-size="9">160×120</text>
  <rect x="275" y="50" width="60" height="90" rx="6" fill="rgba(56,189,248,0.1)" stroke="rgba(56,189,248,0.5)"/>
  <text x="305" y="78" text-anchor="middle" fill="var(--text)" font-size="10">C3</text><text x="305" y="92" text-anchor="middle" fill="var(--text-faint)" font-size="9">512ch</text><text x="305" y="106" text-anchor="middle" fill="var(--text-faint)" font-size="9">80×60</text>
  <rect x="355" y="40" width="45" height="110" rx="6" fill="rgba(56,189,248,0.1)" stroke="rgba(56,189,248,0.5)"/>
  <text x="377.5" y="74" text-anchor="middle" fill="var(--text)" font-size="10">C4</text><text x="377.5" y="88" text-anchor="middle" fill="var(--text-faint)" font-size="9">1024ch</text><text x="377.5" y="102" text-anchor="middle" fill="var(--text-faint)" font-size="9">40×30</text>
  <rect x="420" y="30" width="30" height="130" rx="6" fill="rgba(56,189,248,0.14)" stroke="var(--gold)" stroke-width="1.5"/>
  <text x="435" y="68" text-anchor="middle" fill="var(--text)" font-size="10">C5</text><text x="435" y="82" text-anchor="middle" fill="var(--text-faint)" font-size="9">2048ch</text><text x="435" y="96" text-anchor="middle" fill="var(--text-faint)" font-size="9">20×15</text>
  <text x="463" y="32" fill="var(--gold)" font-size="10">← FiLM HERE</text>
  <text x="50" y="178" fill="var(--text-faint)" font-size="8" font-family="monospace">POPW config: [B×3×480×640] → … → C5 [B×2048×H×W] (see tensor table). Left blocks are schematic, not ImageNet-224 sizes.</text>

  <text x="650" y="36" fill="var(--purple)" font-size="13" font-weight="700">Path B — PoseFiLMModule (model.py)</text>
  <rect x="560" y="52" width="70" height="30" rx="5" fill="rgba(167,139,250,0.12)" stroke="rgba(167,139,250,0.5)"/><text x="595" y="72" text-anchor="middle" fill="var(--text)" font-size="10">pose_flat</text>
  <text x="630" y="68" fill="var(--text-faint)" font-size="9">[B,51]</text>
  <rect x="660" y="48" width="420" height="52" rx="8" fill="rgba(167,139,250,0.08)" stroke="rgba(167,139,250,0.35)"/>
  <text x="870" y="64" text-anchor="middle" fill="var(--text)" font-size="9.5" font-weight="600">γ_net &amp; β_net (separate weights)</text>
  <text x="870" y="78" text-anchor="middle" fill="var(--text-faint)" font-size="8.5" font-family="monospace">Linear(51→512)+ReLU+Linear(512→2048) each</text>
  <text x="870" y="92" text-anchor="middle" fill="var(--purple)" font-size="8.5">γ = 1+tanh(γ_raw) → (0,2); β unbounded</text>
  <text x="640" y="115" fill="var(--teal)" font-size="10">FiLM: γ⊙C5 + β (broadcast over H×W)</text>
  <text x="640" y="132" fill="var(--text-faint)" font-size="9">Confidence for FiLM: softmax heatmap max → sigmoid, computed under torch.no_grad()</text>

  <text x="650" y="190" fill="var(--green)" font-size="13" font-weight="700">Path C — Output heads + Kendall</text>

<thead><tr><th>Stage</th><th>Shape</th><th>Notes</th></tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td>Input RGB</td><td><code>[B, 3, 480, 640]</code></td><td>NCHW; 640×480 display = W×H (matches <code>cv2.resize(..., (img_w, img_h))</code>)</td></tr>
<tr><td>Stem + maxpool</td><td><code>[B, 64, 160, 120]</code></td><td>conv7×7 stride 2, BN, ReLU, then maxpool3×3 stride 2 &mdash; 1/4 spatial</td></tr>
<tr><td>C2 (layer1)</td><td><code>[B, 256, 160, 120]</code></td><td>1/4 resolution, 256 channels</td></tr>
<tr><td>C3 (layer2)</td><td><code>[B, 512, 80, 60]</code></td><td>1/8 resolution, 512 channels</td></tr>
<tr><td>C4 (layer3)</td><td><code>[B, 1024, 40, 30]</code></td><td>1/16 resolution, 1024 channels</td></tr>
<tr><td>C5 (layer4)</td><td><code>[B, 2048, 20, 15]</code></td><td>1/32 resolution, deepest semantics &mdash; feeds FiLM</td></tr>
<tr><td>FPN (neck)</td><td colspan="2" style="font-size:0.9em"><code>model.py</code> <code>FPN.forward(c3,c4,c5)</code> &rarr; <strong>P3&ndash;P7</strong> only. <strong>No P2 level</strong> (C2 is not an FPN input).</td></tr>
<tr><td>FPN P3</td><td><code>[B, 256, 80, 60]</code></td><td>stride 8; pose heatmap head + detection</td></tr>
<tr><td>FPN P4</td><td><code>[B, 256, 40, 30]</code></td><td>stride 16; GAP &rarr; Activity head (fused with GAP(C5_mod))</td></tr>
<tr><td>FPN P5</td><td><code>[B, 256, 20, 15]</code></td><td>stride 32; detection</td></tr>
<tr><td>FPN P6</td><td><code>[B, 256, 10, 8]</code></td><td>stride 64; <code>p6_conv</code> on C5</td></tr>
<tr><td>FPN P7</td><td><code>[B, 256, 5, 4]</code></td><td>stride 128; <code>p7_conv</code> after ReLU(P6)</td></tr>
<tr><td>Heatmaps</td><td><code>[B, 17, 64, 85]</code></td><td>Per-joint pose heatmaps (<code>PoseHead</code> from P3)</td></tr>
<tr><td>pose_flat</td><td><code>[B, 51]</code></td><td>34 coords + 17 confidence values</td></tr>
<tr><td>&gamma;, &beta;</td><td><code>[B, 2048, 1, 1]</code></td><td>Per-channel scale and shift (broadcast)</td></tr>
<tr><td>C5_mod</td><td><code>[B, 2048, 20, 15]</code></td><td>Pose-conditioned features</td></tr>
<tr><td>Activity concat</td><td><code>[B, 2304]</code></td><td>GAP(C5_mod) + GAP(P4)</td></tr>
<tr><td>Activity logits</td><td><code>[B, N_act]</code></td><td><code>N_act = len(ACT_CLASS_NAMES)</code> in <code>config.py</code> (33 fallback if class file missing; else full atomic list + NA)</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</div>

<p style="font-size:0.88rem;color:var(--text-muted);margin:0 0 10px">Grounded in <code>config.py</code> and <code>losses.py</code> (not illustrative).</p>

<!-- ============== SECTION 3: POSE HEATMAP HEAD ============== -->
<section id="pose-head">
<h2><span class="sec-num">03</span> Pose Heatmap Head</h2>
<p class="subtitle">From FPN features to 17 joint heatmaps, soft-argmax, and the 51D pose vector</p>

<div class="card">
<h4>Forward path</h4>
<p>FPN features are decoded with transposed convolutions to a heatmap tensor <code>[B, 17, 64, 85]</code> &mdash; one channel per COCO joint at roughly 1/8 resolution relative to the input width/height layout used in POPW. Each channel is a soft 2D distribution over joint location.</p>
<p><strong>Soft-argmax</strong> extracts differentiable $(x,y)$ per joint from each heatmap (expected value under the normalized softmax). Gradients flow into the heatmap head and thus the backbone for keypoint positions.</p>
<p><strong>Confidences</strong> are taken as the max response per heatmap (or equivalent peak score). Those 17 scalars are detached (<code>torch.no_grad</code>) before concatenation into <code>pose_flat</code> so they act as non-differentiable gating features for FiLM rather than a second path that would shortcut pose supervision.</p>
</div>

<div class="study-viz-wide">
<p class="study-viz-caption"><strong>Study visual · Heatmaps &rarr; vector.</strong> Seventeen separate 2D maps (one joint each); soft-argmax turns each map into $(x,y)$; peak scores become confidences. The stick figure is the same COCO topology you pack into <code>pose_flat</code>.</p>
<svg viewBox="0 0 720 200" role="img" aria-label="From 17 heatmaps and a skeleton to a 51 dimensional pose vector">
  <defs>
    <radialGradient id="hmBlob" cx="50%" cy="45%" r="55%"><stop offset="0%" stop-color="var(--teal)" stop-opacity="0.95"/><stop offset="100%" stop-color="var(--teal)" stop-opacity="0.08"/></radialGradient>
    <marker id="hmArr" markerWidth="7" markerHeight="5" refX="7" refY="2.5" orient="auto"><path d="M0,0 L7,2.5 L0,5Z" fill="var(--text-muted)"/></marker>
  </defs>
  <text x="360" y="18" text-anchor="middle" fill="var(--text)" font-size="12" font-weight="700">17 channels &middot; soft-argmax per joint &middot; + 17 confidences = 51D</text>
  <g transform="translate(24,40)">
    <rect x="0" y="0" width="52" height="72" rx="6" fill="var(--surface)" stroke="var(--border)" stroke-width="1.2"/>
    <ellipse cx="26" cy="28" rx="14" ry="10" fill="url(#hmBlob)"/>
    <text x="26" y="88" text-anchor="middle" fill="var(--text-faint)" font-size="8" font-family="var(--font-mono),monospace">ch 9</text>

    <path d="M48 22 L48 52 M36 36 L60 36 M36 36 L28 50 M60 36 L72 24 M48 52 L40 72 M48 52 L56 72" stroke="#1e293b" stroke-width="5" fill="none"/>
    <path d="M48 22 L48 52 M36 36 L60 36 M36 36 L28 50 M60 36 L72 24 M48 52 L40 72 M48 52 L56 72" stroke="var(--surface)" stroke-width="2.4" fill="none"/>
    <circle cx="48" cy="22" r="7" fill="var(--teal)" stroke="#0f172a" stroke-width="1"/>
    <circle cx="36" cy="36" r="4" fill="var(--teal)"/><circle cx="60" cy="36" r="4" fill="var(--teal)"/><circle cx="72" cy="24" r="4" fill="var(--gold)"/>
  </g>
  <line x1="340" y1="76" x2="388" y2="76" stroke="var(--text-muted)" stroke-width="1.2" marker-end="url(#hmArr)"/>
  <rect x="396" y="36" width="200" height="80" rx="8" fill="var(--surface-2)" stroke="var(--purple)" stroke-width="1.3"/>
  <text x="496" y="58" text-anchor="middle" fill="var(--purple)" font-size="11" font-weight="700" font-family="var(--font-mono),monospace">pose_flat [B, 51]</text>
  <text x="496" y="78" text-anchor="middle" fill="var(--text-muted)" font-size="9" font-family="var(--font-body),sans-serif">34 coords + 17 conf (detached)</text>
  <text x="496" y="100" text-anchor="middle" fill="var(--gold)" font-size="8.5" font-weight="600">&rarr; FiLM MLP (§4)</text>
  <line x1="604" y1="76" x2="652" y2="76" stroke="var(--text-muted)" stroke-width="1.2" marker-end="url(#hmArr)"/>
  <rect x="660" y="48" width="52" height="56" rx="8" fill="rgba(251,191,36,0.12)" stroke="var(--gold)" stroke-width="1.5"/>
  <text x="686" y="72" text-anchor="middle" fill="var(--gold)" font-size="9" font-weight="700">FiLM</text>
  <text x="686" y="88" text-anchor="middle" fill="var(--text-faint)" font-size="7">on C5</text>
</svg>
</div>

<h3>Why heatmaps instead of direct regression?</h3>
<p>Spatial heatmaps preserve <strong>spatial uncertainty</strong> (multi-modal peaks under occlusion) and match standard pose-estimation literature (HRNet, ViTPose). Soft-argmax keeps the pipeline end-to-end differentiable while avoiding hard argmax.</p>

<h3>Interactive: toy heatmap &rarr; soft coordinate</h3>
<p>2D Gaussian bump; slider moves the peak. Soft-argmax (normalized exp weighted sum) yields the displayed $(x,y)$ in grid coordinates. <span style="color:var(--text-muted)">Teaching toy only — not exported POPW heatmaps.</span></p>
<div class="interactive-explorer">
  <div class="slider-group">
    <label>Peak x</label>
    <input type="range" id="hmX" min="5" max="55" value="30" oninput="drawHeatmapSoftmax()">
    <span class="slider-val" id="hmXVal">30</span>
  </div>
  <div class="slider-group">
    <label>Peak y</label>
    <input type="range" id="hmY" min="5" max="35" value="18" oninput="drawHeatmapSoftmax()">
    <span class="slider-val" id="hmYVal">18</span>
  </div>
  <div class="heatmap-canvas-wrap"><canvas id="hmCanvas" width="240" height="160" role="img" aria-label="Toy Gaussian heatmap and soft-argmax coordinates"></canvas></div>
  <p class="mono" id="hmSoftArgmax" style="font-size:0.85rem;color:var(--teal)">soft-argmax (x, y) = &hellip;</p>
</div>

<div class="definition-box">
<h4>PCKh@0.5 Metric Definition</h4>
$$\text{PCKh@0.5} = \frac{\#\text{keypoints where } \|p_i - g_i\|_2 \leq 0.5 \times \text{head\_size}}{\#\text{total keypoints}}$$
<p>where $\text{head\_size} = \|p_{\text{head\_top}} - p_{\text{neck}}\|_2$ is the Euclidean distance between the top of the head and the neck joint in ground truth. This normalizes the distance threshold by body scale, making the metric robust across subjects at different distances from the camera.</p>
<p>Our <code>compare_results.json</code> eval uses normalized coordinates: improved4 achieves <strong>99.53%</strong> at PCK@0.05 and <strong>99.89%</strong> at PCK@0.01; improved4_film is <strong>99.58%</strong> / <strong>99.89%</strong> at the same thresholds.</p>
</div>

<div class="definition-box" style="border-color:var(--purple);">
<h4 style="color:var(--purple);">Why BCE, not softmax Cross-Entropy for joint confidence?</h4>
<p>Each joint's confidence is an <strong>independent binary question</strong>: "Is this joint visible/detected?" This is modeled as $p(c_j = 1)$ per joint $j$, trained with Binary Cross-Entropy (BCE):</p>
$$\mathcal{L}_{\text{conf}} = -\frac{1}{17}\sum_{j=1}^{17}\left[y_j \log(\hat{c}_j) + (1-y_j)\log(1-\hat{c}_j)\right]$$
<p>Softmax CE would force competition between joints &mdash; detecting one joint would suppress another since softmax outputs sum to 1. BCE treats each joint as an i.i.d. Bernoulli trial: detecting the left wrist has zero effect on the right ankle's confidence. This is both physically correct (joint visibility is independent) and mathematically necessary (we want all 17 confidences to be simultaneously high when all joints are visible).</p>
</div>
</section>

<!-- ============== SECTION 4: FiLM MODULE ============== -->
<section id="film" data-novelty="1">
<div class="novelty-strip prof-badge">NOVELTY: pose-conditioned FiLM on RGB-CNN features</div>
<h2><span class="sec-num">04</span> FiLM Module</h2>
<p class="subtitle">The core architectural novelty &mdash; deep mathematical breakdown</p>
<span class="badge badge-novelty prof-badge">OUR NOVELTY</span>

<div class="card card-cite">
<h4>Origin Paper</h4>
<p><strong>"FiLM: Visual Reasoning with a General Conditioning Layer"</strong><br>
Perez et al., AAAI 2018 &middot; <a href="https://arxiv.org/abs/1709.07871" target="_blank" rel="noopener">arxiv.org/abs/1709.07871</a></p>
<p style="color:var(--text-muted); margin-bottom:0"><strong>Our adaptation:</strong> Instead of conditioning on language (as in the original VQA application), we condition on human pose geometry encoded as a 51-dimensional skeleton vector. No prior work applies FiLM conditioning with pose as the conditioning signal for action recognition (see Section 12).</p>
</div>

<h3>4a. The Core Equation</h3>
<p>FiLM applies a learned, input-conditioned affine transformation per feature channel:</p>

$$\text{FiLM}(\mathbf{F}_i \mid \mathbf{z}) = \gamma_i(\mathbf{z}) \cdot \mathbf{F}_i + \beta_i(\mathbf{z})$$

<div class="study-viz-wide">
<p class="study-viz-caption"><strong>Study visual · FiLM dataflow.</strong> The stickman is the RGB-frame proxy: pixels build <span class="mono">C5 raw</span>; pose extracted from that same frame gives <span class="mono">z</span>; FiLM uses <span class="mono">z</span> to modulate C5 (not to replace image features).</p>

<div class="table-wrap">
<table>
<thead><tr><th>Symbol</th><th>Type</th><th>Shape</th><th>Range</th><th>Physical Meaning</th><th>Design Rationale</th></tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td>$\mathbf{F}_i$</td><td>Input</td><td><code>[B, 2048, 20, 15]</code></td><td>Real-valued</td><td>The $i$-th channel of ResNet-50's C5 feature map</td><td>C5 is the deepest semantic feature &mdash; conditioning here modulates the highest-level visual semantics</td></tr>
<tr><td>$\gamma_i$</td><td>Scale</td><td><code>[B, 2048]</code></td><td>$(0, 2)$</td><td>Multiplicative gain for channel $i$. $\gamma > 1$ amplifies; $\gamma < 1$ suppresses</td><td>Allows pose to say "pay more attention to this visual pattern"</td></tr>
<tr><td>$\beta_i$</td><td>Shift</td><td><code>[B, 2048]</code></td><td>Unbounded</td><td>Additive bias for channel $i$</td><td>Promotes features even without strong visual evidence, given body position</td></tr>
<tr><td>$\mathbf{z}$</td><td>Condition</td><td><code>[B, 51]</code></td><td>$[0,1]$</td><td>51D pose vector: 34 normalized (x,y) + 17 confidence scores</td><td>Compact, differentiable body configuration encoding</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</div>

<h3>4b. Why Exactly 2048 &gamma; and &beta; Values?</h3>
<div class="card">
<p>ResNet-50's C5 (the final convolutional stage) outputs <code>[B, 2048, 20, 15]</code> at our 640&times;480 input. The 2048 channel count comes from ResNet-50's bottleneck design: the final expansion layer has 4&times; the base width (512 &times; 4 = 2048). FiLM operates <strong>channel-wise</strong> &mdash; each $\gamma_i$ and $\beta_i$ scales/shifts exactly one channel &mdash; so we need exactly <strong>2048 each</strong>.</p>
</div>

<h3>4c. Activation Functions: Why 1+tanh for &gamma;, Unbounded for &beta;</h3>
<p><strong>For &gamma; (scale):</strong> $\gamma_i = 1 + \tanh(\text{raw}_i) \in (0, 2)$</p>
<p>Four purposes: (1) <strong>Identity initialization</strong> &mdash; at init when raw &asymp; 0, &gamma; = 1.0. (2) <strong>Bounded amplification</strong> &mdash; max 2.0&times;. (3) <strong>Bounded suppression</strong> &mdash; approaches but never reaches 0. (4) <strong>No polarity inversion</strong> &mdash; &gamma; &gt; 0 always.</p>
<p><strong>For &beta; (shift):</strong> $\beta_i = \text{raw}_i$ &mdash; unbounded linear output. No risk of sign-flip instability.</p>
<p style="font-size:0.88rem;color:var(--text-muted)"><strong>Why not $2\sigma(\cdot)$ for &gamma;?</strong> A sigmoid maps to $(0,1)$; doubling gives $(0,2)$ with identity near $\sigma=0.5$. That only <em>suppresses</em> toward 0 or <em>amplifies</em> toward 2 from a 1.0 baseline in a fixed sigmoid shape. <strong>$1+\tanh$</strong> is symmetric around 0 in the latent: negative logits smoothly suppress below 1, positive logits amplify above 1, with bounded derivatives. Either is valid; we follow the interactive explorer above for intuition.</p>
<p style="font-size:0.88rem;color:var(--text-muted)"><strong>Hidden layers: ReLU vs tanh for &beta; MLP?</strong> The <em>output</em> &beta; is linear. Internally we use ReLU for capacity; if the <em>last</em> nonlinearity on &beta; were ReLU-only, the <em>bias</em> path from negative pre-activations would be clipped to 0 &mdash; asymmetric. Keeping a final <strong>linear</strong> head on &beta; avoids forcing all shifts nonnegative.</p>

<h3>4c&#8321;. Interactive: activation outputs vs. latent logit <span class="badge badge-claim prof-badge">KEY CLAIM</span></h3>
<div class="interactive-explorer" id="activationExplorer">
  <h4>Latent logit <span class="mono">z</span> (one channel before nonlinearity)</h4>
  <div class="slider-group">
    <label>z</label>
    <input type="range" id="actZSlider" min="-300" max="300" value="0" oninput="updateActivationExplorer()">
    <span class="slider-val" id="actZVal">0.00</span>
  </div>
  <div class="viz-row"><span class="viz-label">&sigma;(z) &isin; (0,1)</span><div class="viz-bar-track"><div class="viz-bar-fill" id="barSigma" style="width:50%;background:var(--orange)"></div></div></div>
  <div class="viz-row"><span class="viz-label">tanh(z) &isin; (&minus;1,1)</span><div class="viz-bar-track"><div class="viz-bar-fill" id="barTanh" style="width:50%;background:var(--purple)"></div></div></div>
  <div class="viz-row"><span class="viz-label">1+tanh(z) = &gamma;</span><div class="viz-bar-track"><div class="viz-bar-fill" id="barGammaAct" style="width:50%;background:var(--green)"></div></div></div>
  <p style="font-size:0.78rem;color:var(--text-muted);margin:12px 0 0">Bars show value mapped to 0&ndash;2 scale (50% = 1.0).</p>
</div>

<h3>4d. FiLM MLP Architecture (from code)</h3>
<div class="arch-flow-wrap">
<svg viewBox="0 0 700 320" width="100%" style="max-width:700px;display:block;" role="img" aria-label="FiLM MLP internal architecture diagram">
  <defs>
    <marker id="mlp-a" markerWidth="7" markerHeight="5" refX="7" refY="2.5" orient="auto"><path d="M0,0 L7,2.5 L0,5Z" fill="var(--text-muted)"/></marker>

<p><strong>Parameter count:</strong> Per branch: Linear1 $51 \times 512 + 512 = 26{,}624$ &middot; Linear2 $512 \times 2048 + 2048 = 1{,}050{,}624$ &rarr; <strong>1,077,248</strong> per net. Both nets &rarr; <strong>2,154,496</strong> FiLM params (<strong>+5.4%</strong> vs 40.10M backbone — still &lt;6% of total trainable weights).</p>

<h3>4e&#8321;. Scalar intuition: fixed feature $F=1$</h3>
<p style="font-size:0.85rem;color:var(--text-muted)">FiLM does $\tilde F = \gamma F + \beta$ per channel. With $F=1$ and the <em>slider</em> scale factors $g,b$ (same mapping as §4f), output is $g + b$.</p>
<div class="table-wrap">
<table>
<thead><tr><th>&gamma; scale $g$</th><th>Effect on $F=1$</th></tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td>$0$</td><td>Silences channel (0)</td></tr>
<tr><td>$0.5$</td><td>Half amplitude vs identity-at-1</td></tr>
<tr><td>$1$</td><td>Identity scaling (before &beta;)</td></tr>
<tr><td>$1.5$</td><td>50% amplification</td></tr>
<tr><td>$2$</td><td>Max bounded scale in $1+\tanh$ design</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</div>
<div class="table-wrap">
<table>
<thead><tr><th>&beta; shift $b$</th><th>$\tilde F$ with $F=1$, $g=1$</th></tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td>$0$</td><td>$1.0$</td></tr>
<tr><td>$+0.4$</td><td>$1.4$</td></tr>
<tr><td>$-0.4$</td><td>$0.6$</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</div>

<section id="pose-vector">
<h2><span class="sec-num">05</span> Pose Vector: Why 51 Dimensions?</h2>
<p class="subtitle">Decomposing the conditioning signal that drives FiLM</p>

<div class="card" style="margin-bottom:20px;border-color:rgba(56,189,248,0.25);">
<h4 style="margin-top:0;color:var(--teal)">Living frame · pose tensor on the RGB canvas</h4>
<p style="font-size:0.85rem;color:var(--text-muted);margin-bottom:10px">Large panel for lecture mode: step through RGB → skeleton (all joints) → C5 grid → FiLM overlay.</p>

<div class="living-frame-wrap" data-lf-instance="frame-film-math">

<div class="table-wrap">
<table>
<thead><tr><th>Dims</th><th>Keypoints</th><th>Body Region</th><th>Why These Joints?</th></tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><code>0&ndash;1</code></td><td>Nose</td><td>Head</td><td>Head orientation &rarr; attention direction</td></tr>
<tr><td><code>2&ndash;5</code></td><td>Left Eye, Right Eye</td><td>Head</td><td>Gaze direction disambiguation</td></tr>
<tr><td><code>6&ndash;9</code></td><td>Left Ear, Right Ear</td><td>Head</td><td>Head rotation (profile vs. frontal)</td></tr>
<tr><td><code>10&ndash;13</code></td><td>Left Shoulder, Right Shoulder</td><td>Torso</td><td>Primary assembly pivot points &mdash; shoulder angles define reach geometry</td></tr>
<tr><td><code>14&ndash;17</code></td><td>Left Elbow, Right Elbow</td><td>Arms</td><td>Elbow angle encodes reach distance and height</td></tr>
<tr><td><code>18&ndash;21</code></td><td>Left Wrist, Right Wrist</td><td>Hands</td><td><strong>Most discriminative</strong> for manipulation phase</td></tr>
<tr><td><code>22&ndash;25</code></td><td>Left Hip, Right Hip</td><td>Core</td><td>Body inclination (bending vs. upright)</td></tr>
<tr><td><code>26&ndash;29</code></td><td>Left Knee, Right Knee</td><td>Legs</td><td>Standing vs. crouching vs. kneeling posture</td></tr>
<tr><td><code>30&ndash;33</code></td><td>Left Ankle, Right Ankle</td><td>Legs</td><td>Full body support configuration</td></tr>
<tr><td><code>34&ndash;50</code></td><td>Confidence &times; 17</td><td>All</td><td>Per-joint confidence (0&ndash;1) from heatmap maxima, under <code>no_grad</code></td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</div>

<p><strong>Total:</strong> 17 joints &times; 2 coordinates (x, y normalized to [0,1]) = 34 values + 17 confidence scores = <strong>51 dimensions</strong>.</p>
<p><strong>Why (x, y) not (x, y, z)?</strong> Monocular RGB &mdash; depth is not directly available. 2D projected positions are normalized by feature map resolution to [0, 1].</p>
<p><strong>Why COCO 17?</strong> Standard skeleton used by all major pose estimators (HRNet, ViTPose, OpenPose). 17 keypoints provide sufficient body configuration detail for assembly actions while ensuring compatibility with pretrained models.</p>

<h3>5d. 51D vector visualization</h3>
<p style="font-size:0.82rem;color:var(--text-muted)">51 values = 17 joints × 3 coordinates. Green border highlights wrist dimensions (most discriminative in assembly).</p>

<h3>5f. Why 51D is sufficient (flat encoding vs pyramid)</h3>
<svg viewBox="0 0 980 300" style="width:100%;border:1px solid var(--border);border-radius:10px;background:var(--surface);">
  <text x="245" y="28" text-anchor="middle" fill="var(--orange)" font-size="13" font-weight="700">Pyramid approach (not what we use)</text>
  <rect x="40" y="45" width="410" height="230" rx="8" fill="rgba(251,146,60,0.08)" stroke="rgba(251,146,60,0.35)"/>
  <rect x="75" y="70" width="340" height="34" rx="5" fill="rgba(148,163,184,0.15)" stroke="rgba(148,163,184,0.5)"/>
  <text x="245" y="91" text-anchor="middle" fill="var(--text)" font-size="11">P3 [256×80×60]</text>
  <rect x="105" y="112" width="280" height="30" rx="5" fill="rgba(148,163,184,0.15)" stroke="rgba(148,163,184,0.5)"/>
  <text x="245" y="131" text-anchor="middle" fill="var(--text)" font-size="11">P4 [256×40×30]</text>
  <rect x="135" y="150" width="220" height="27" rx="5" fill="rgba(148,163,184,0.15)" stroke="rgba(148,163,184,0.5)"/>
  <text x="245" y="168" text-anchor="middle" fill="var(--text)" font-size="11">P5 [256×20×15]</text>
  <rect x="165" y="184" width="160" height="24" rx="5" fill="rgba(148,163,184,0.15)" stroke="rgba(148,163,184,0.5)"/>
  <text x="245" y="200" text-anchor="middle" fill="var(--text)" font-size="10">P6–P7 …</text>
  <text x="245" y="248" text-anchor="middle" fill="var(--orange)" font-size="10">4× more parameters, 4× more complexity</text>

  <text x="735" y="28" text-anchor="middle" fill="var(--teal)" font-size="13" font-weight="700">Flat approach (our design)</text>
  <rect x="530" y="45" width="410" height="230" rx="8" fill="rgba(56,189,248,0.08)" stroke="rgba(56,189,248,0.35)"/>
  <rect x="575" y="80" width="120" height="38" rx="6" fill="rgba(56,189,248,0.14)" stroke="rgba(56,189,248,0.45)"/>
  <text x="635" y="104" text-anchor="middle" fill="var(--text)" font-size="11">p [51D]</text>
  <line x1="695" y1="99" x2="750" y2="99" stroke="var(--text-muted)" stroke-width="1.2"/>
  <rect x="750" y="80" width="120" height="38" rx="6" fill="rgba(167,139,250,0.14)" stroke="rgba(167,139,250,0.45)"/>
  <text x="810" y="104" text-anchor="middle" fill="var(--text)" font-size="11">MLP</text>
  <line x1="810" y1="118" x2="810" y2="160" stroke="var(--text-muted)" stroke-width="1.2"/>
  <rect x="750" y="160" width="120" height="38" rx="6" fill="rgba(74,222,128,0.12)" stroke="rgba(74,222,128,0.45)"/>
  <text x="810" y="184" text-anchor="middle" fill="var(--text)" font-size="11">γ/β [2048]</text>
  <line x1="750" y1="179" x2="700" y2="209" stroke="var(--text-muted)" stroke-width="1.2"/>
  <rect x="600" y="200" width="120" height="36" rx="6" fill="rgba(56,189,248,0.16)" stroke="rgba(56,189,248,0.55)"/>
  <text x="660" y="222" text-anchor="middle" fill="var(--text)" font-size="11">Apply to C5 only</text>
  <text x="735" y="248" text-anchor="middle" fill="var(--teal)" font-size="10">1 set of parameters, semantic conditioning only</text>
</svg>
<p style="font-size:0.82rem;color:var(--text-muted)">We condition only C5 because it carries semantic "what" information; C1–C3 mostly encode edges/textures that are largely pose-invariant. Multi-scale conditioning increases complexity with diminishing returns.</p>
</section>

<div class="card card-cite">
<h4>Origin Paper</h4>
<p><strong>"Wing Loss for Robust Facial Landmark Localisation with Convolutional Neural Networks"</strong><br>
Feng et al., CVPR 2018 &middot; <a href="https://arxiv.org/abs/1711.06753" target="_blank" rel="noopener">arxiv.org/abs/1711.06753</a></p>
<p style="color:var(--text-muted); margin-bottom:0"><strong>Our adaptation:</strong> Originally designed for 68 facial landmarks in roughly 80px face regions. We apply it to 17 body keypoints in a 640&times;480 image space &mdash; a much larger spatial extent with different error scales.</p>
</div>

<h3>6a. Four Loss Functions Compared</h3>

<p><strong>L2 Loss (MSE):</strong> $\mathcal{L}_{L2}(x) = x^2$ &mdash; Gradient $2x$ grows without bound. Outliers dominate training.</p>

<p><strong>L1 Loss (MAE):</strong> $\mathcal{L}_{L1}(x) = |x|$ &mdash; Gradient $\pm 1$ everywhere. Small errors get no extra signal.</p>

<p><strong>Huber Loss:</strong></p>
$$\mathcal{L}_{\text{Huber}}(x) = \begin{cases} \frac{1}{2}x^2 & \text{if } |x| < \delta \\ \delta(|x| - \frac{\delta}{2}) & \text{otherwise} \end{cases}$$
<p>Fixed global &delta; cannot be optimal for all joints simultaneously.</p>

<p><strong>Wing Loss (ours):</strong></p>
$$\mathcal{L}_{\text{Wing}}(x) = \begin{cases} w \cdot \ln\left(1 + \frac{|x|}{\epsilon}\right) & \text{if } |x| < w \\ |x| - C & \text{otherwise} \end{cases}$$
<p>where $C = w - w \cdot \ln\left(1 + \frac{w}{\epsilon}\right)$ ensures continuity at $|x| = w$.</p>

<div class="card card-accent">
<h4 style="margin-top:0">Deriving $C$ at the boundary $|x| = w$</h4>
<p style="font-size:0.88rem">For $x &gt; 0$, the log branch gives $L_{\log}(w) = w\ln(1 + w/\epsilon)$. The linear branch is $L_{\mathrm{lin}}(x) = x - C$. Require <strong>equal values</strong> at $x = w$: $w\ln(1 + w/\epsilon) = w - C$, hence $C = w - w\ln(1 + w/\epsilon)$.</p>
<p style="font-size:0.88rem;margin-bottom:0">Require <strong>equal derivatives</strong> (smooth join): left derivative at $w$ is $\frac{d}{dx}\big[w\ln(1+x/\epsilon)\big]_{x=w} = \frac{w}{\epsilon + w}$; right derivative is $1$. These are <em>not</em> equal in general — Wing uses a <strong>continuous</strong> (C⁰) join; the kink still yields subgradient behavior suitable for optimization. The constant $C$ removes the <em>jump</em> in loss values that would occur if the linear tail were $x$ without offset.</p>
</div>

<h3>6b. Our Parameters</h3>
<div class="table-wrap">
<table>
<thead><tr><th>Parameter</th><th>Value</th><th>Physical Meaning</th><th>Why This Value</th></tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td>$w$ (omega)</td><td><code>0.05</code></td><td>Threshold separating log (small error) and linear (large error) regimes, in normalized coordinates</td><td>0.05 of normalized [0,1] range &asymp; 32px at 640px width &mdash; natural precision threshold for body keypoints</td></tr>
<tr><td>$\epsilon$</td><td><code>0.005</code></td><td>Controls curvature of the log region. Smaller &epsilon; &rarr; steeper gradient for tiny errors</td><td>&epsilon; = 0.005 gives gradient $w/\epsilon = 10$ at $x=0$, providing 10&times; stronger signal than L1 for subpixel corrections</td></tr>
<tr><td>$C$</td><td>Derived</td><td>Continuity constant ensuring no jump at $x = w$</td><td>$C = w(1 - \ln(1 + w/\epsilon))$ &mdash; automatically computed</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</div>

<h3>6c. Gradient Analysis &mdash; The Key Insight</h3>
$$\frac{d\mathcal{L}_{\text{Wing}}}{dx} = \begin{cases} \frac{w}{\epsilon + |x|} & \text{if } |x| < w \\ \text{sign}(x) & \text{otherwise} \end{cases}$$

<div class="table-wrap">
<table>
<thead><tr><th>Error $|x|$</th><th>L2 gradient</th><th>L1 gradient</th><th>Huber (&delta;=0.01)</th><th>Wing (w=0.05, &epsilon;=0.005)</th></tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td>0.001 (subpixel)</td><td>0.002</td><td>1.0</td><td>0.001</td><td style="color:var(--green)"><strong>8.33</strong></td></tr>
<tr><td>0.01 (1%)</td><td>0.02</td><td>1.0</td><td>1.0</td><td style="color:var(--green)"><strong>3.33</strong></td></tr>
<tr><td>0.05 (threshold)</td><td>0.10</td><td>1.0</td><td>1.0</td><td style="color:var(--teal)"><strong>0.91</strong></td></tr>
<tr><td>0.20 (outlier)</td><td>0.40</td><td>1.0</td><td>1.0</td><td><strong>1.0</strong></td></tr>
<tr><td>0.03125 (≈20px @ 640w)</td><td>0.0625</td><td>1.0</td><td>1.0</td><td style="color:var(--teal)"><strong>1.11</strong></td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</div>
<p>Wing loss provides <strong>higher gradient for small errors</strong> (driving precision) and <strong>equal gradient for large errors</strong> (matching L1's outlier resistance). This is exactly what precision-critical keypoint tasks need.</p>

<h3>6f. Worst Case Without Wing Loss</h3>
<p style="font-size:0.88rem;color:var(--text-muted)">L2 lets rare occluded joints steal the pose gradient; Wing caps large-error slope at 1 while amplifying sub-threshold corrections.</p>
<p><button type="button" class="hdr-btn" onclick="var p=document.getElementById('wingWithoutPanel'); if(p){ p.hidden=!p.hidden; this.setAttribute('aria-expanded', p.hidden?'false':'true'); }" aria-expanded="false">Show without Wing</button></p>
<div id="wingWithoutPanel" class="without-method-panel" hidden>
<div class="card card-warn" style="border:none;background:transparent;padding:0;box-shadow:none">
<h4 style="color:var(--orange)">If we used L2 Loss instead:</h4>
<p>A single frame where the wrist keypoint detector fails badly (e.g., occlusion by assembly part &mdash; common in IKEA ASM) generates squared error that dominates the gradient. The model spends disproportionate update budget on outlier detections rather than refining already-good predictions. This is catastrophic in IKEA assembly where <strong>object occlusion causes systematic outlier keypoints</strong>.</p>

<h3>6g. Professor Questions</h3>
<div class="accordion">
  <div class="accordion-item">
    <button class="accordion-trigger" onclick="this.parentElement.classList.toggle('open')">Q: "Why not just use Huber loss?"</button>
    <div class="accordion-body">
      <p>Huber has a fixed global &delta; threshold and provides <strong>quadratic gradient</strong> for small errors &mdash; meaning gradient <em>decreases</em> toward zero as error shrinks. Wing's log region provides <strong>stronger gradient for small errors</strong> than Huber's quadratic region.</p>
      <p>Concrete comparison at $|x| = 0.001$ with &delta; = 0.01:</p>
      <p>&bull; <strong>Huber gradient:</strong> $|x| = 0.001$ (inside quadratic region, gradient = $x = 0.001$)</p>
      <p>&bull; <strong>Wing gradient:</strong> $w/(\epsilon + |x|) = 0.05/(0.005 + 0.001) = \mathbf{8.33}$</p>
      <p>Wing delivers <strong>8,330&times; stronger gradient</strong> at subpixel errors. Wing is specifically designed for keypoint precision where the last fraction of a pixel matters for pose accuracy feeding into FiLM.</p>
    </div>
  </div>
  <div class="accordion-item">
    <button class="accordion-trigger" onclick="this.parentElement.classList.toggle('open')">Q: "How was w=0.05 chosen? Why not w=10?"</button>
    <div class="accordion-body">
      <p>The value $w = 0.05$ is in <strong>normalized coordinates</strong> (keypoint positions are normalized to [0, 1]). In pixel space at 640px width, this equals $0.05 \times 640 \approx 32\text{px}$.</p>
      <p>This threshold separates two regimes:</p>
      <p>&bull; <strong>Precision zone</strong> ($|x| < 0.05$): subpixel corrections get log-amplified gradient. These are the "almost right" joints that need to be pushed to exact positions for accurate FiLM conditioning.</p>
      <p>&bull; <strong>Outlier zone</strong> ($|x| \geq 0.05$): linear L1-like gradient prevents outlier keypoints (occluded, mis-detected) from dominating training with quadratic explosion.</p>
      <p>The value was selected based on the natural precision boundary for body keypoints at our 640&times;480 resolution &mdash; 32px is approximately the width of a wrist or ankle in a standing person at typical IKEA ASM camera distance. Errors smaller than a joint size need precision refinement; errors larger than a joint size are likely detection failures that should be treated as outliers.</p>
    </div>
  </div>
</div>
</section>
<!-- ============== SECTION 7: ACTIVITY HEAD ============== -->
<section id="activity-head">
<h2><span class="sec-num">07</span> Activity Head: CB-Focal Loss</h2>
<p class="subtitle">Solving 33-class imbalance with class-balanced focal down-weighting</p>

<div class="study-viz-wide">
<p class="study-viz-caption"><strong>Study visual · RGB stickman to CB-Focal.</strong> One frame can map to a common or rare class; CB-Focal forces learning to not collapse on frequent actions.</p>

</svg>
</div>
