<div class="card card-cite">
<h4>Origin Papers</h4>
<p><strong>"Class-Balanced Loss Based on Effective Number of Samples"</strong> — Cui et al., CVPR 2019 · <a href="https://arxiv.org/abs/1901.05555" target="_blank" rel="noopener">arxiv.org/abs/1901.05555</a></p>
<p><strong>"Focal Loss for Dense Object Detection"</strong> — Lin et al., ICCV 2017 · <a href="https://arxiv.org/abs/1708.02002" target="_blank" rel="noopener">arxiv.org/abs/1708.02002</a></p>
<p style="color:var(--text-muted); margin-bottom:0"><strong>Our contribution:</strong> We combine both into a unified CB-Focal Loss, applying class-balanced re-weighting AND focal down-weighting simultaneously. Neither paper alone solves both the class imbalance AND easy-sample-dominance problems present in IKEA ASM's 33-class action set.</p>
</div>

<h3>7b. Three-Stage Equation Build-Up</h3>
<p style="font-size:0.82rem;color:var(--text-muted)">Staged reveal (professor pacing):</p>
<div style="display:flex;flex-wrap:wrap;gap:8px;margin:8px 0 16px">
<button type="button" class="hdr-btn" onclick="showCbStage(1)">Show CE Problem</button>
<button type="button" class="hdr-btn" onclick="showCbStage(2)">Add CB Weighting</button>
<button type="button" class="hdr-btn" onclick="showCbStage(3)">Add Focal Term</button>
</div>

<div id="formal-problem-block">
<div id="cbStageBlock1">
<h4>Stage 1 — Standard Cross-Entropy</h4>
$$\mathcal{L}_{\text{CE}}(p, y) = -\log(p_y)$$
<p>Treats every sample equally regardless of class frequency. A model that always predicts the most common class can achieve deceptively high accuracy.</p>
<div class="card card-danger" style="padding:10px;margin-top:8px;">
  <strong style="color:var(--red)">CE failure mode:</strong>
  <span style="font-size:0.85rem;">Always predicting <code>Lay (frame)</code> yields 520/3400 = <strong>15.3%</strong> accuracy without learning assembly semantics.</span>
</div>
</div>

<div id="cbStageBlock2" style="display:none">
<h4>Stage 2 — Add Class-Balanced Weighting</h4>
$$\alpha_c = \frac{1 - \beta_{\text{CB}}}{1 - \beta_{\text{CB}}^{n_c}}$$
$$\mathcal{L}_{\text{CB}}(p, y) = -\alpha_y \log(p_y)$$

<div class="table-wrap">
<table>
<thead><tr><th>Symbol</th><th>Value</th><th>Meaning</th></tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td>$\beta_{\text{CB}}$</td><td><code>0.9999</code></td><td>Controls weight decrease rate as class size grows. β→1: inverse-frequency. β→0: no weighting. β=0.9999 gives "effective number of samples" weighting.</td></tr>
<tr><td>$n_c$</td><td>varies</td><td>Training sample count for class $c$</td></tr>
<tr><td>$\alpha_c$</td><td>computed</td><td>Re-weighting factor — higher for rare classes, lower for common classes</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</div>
</div>

<div id="cbStageBlock3" style="display:none">
<h4>Stage 3 — Add Focal Down-weighting</h4>
$$\mathcal{L}_{\text{CB-Focal}}(p, y) = -\alpha_y (1 - p_y)^{\gamma_{\text{focal}}} \log(p_y)$$

<p>When the model is 95% confident on a sample ($p_y = 0.95$): focal factor = $(0.05)^2 = 0.0025$. That sample's loss is reduced 400×, freeing gradient capacity for hard, uncertain samples.</p>

<div class="table-wrap">
<table>
<thead><tr><th>Symbol</th><th>Value</th><th>Meaning</th></tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td>$(1-p_y)^{\gamma_{\text{focal}}}$</td><td>computed</td><td>Down-weighting factor for easy samples</td></tr>
<tr><td>$\gamma_{\text{focal}}$</td><td><code>2.0</code></td><td>Focal exponent. γ=0: standard CE. γ=2: standard focal from Lin et al. (2017)</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</div>
</div>
</div>

<h4 style="margin-top:20px">Focal factor $(1-p)^\gamma$ for different $\gamma$ (at fixed $p$)</h4>
<p style="font-size:0.8rem;color:var(--text-muted)">Larger $\gamma$ more aggressively down-weights easy examples (high $p_y$). $\gamma=2$ is the RetinaNet default; $\gamma=0$ recovers no focal modulation.</p>
<div class="chart-wrap" style="height:260px;min-height:240px">
<canvas id="focalGammaChart"></canvas>
</div>

<h3>7c. Interactive Class Weight Calculator</h3>
<div class="card">
<p style="font-size:0.85rem; margin-bottom:12px">Enter a class sample count to compute the CB weight $\alpha_c = \frac{1 - \beta}{1 - \beta^{n_c}}$ where $\beta = 0.9999$:</p>
<div class="calc-row">
  <label style="font-size:0.85rem">$n_c$ =</label>
  <input class="calc-input" type="number" id="ncInput" value="180" min="1" max="10000" oninput="updateCBCalc()">
  <span style="font-size:0.85rem; color:var(--text-muted)">→ α_c =</span>
  <span class="calc-result" id="alphaResult">0.00605</span>
</div>
<p style="font-size:0.75rem; color:var(--text-muted)" id="cbExplanation">Effective number of samples: 165.27. This class gets 0.00605 weight.</p>
<p style="font-size:0.75rem; color:var(--text-faint); margin-bottom:0">Try: NA class (n≈3000) → α≈0.00033 vs flip class (n≈50) → α≈0.02000. Rare classes receive roughly <strong>60×</strong> more weight.</p>
</div>

<h4 style="margin-top:16px">Effective-number mass $(1-\beta^{n_c})/(1-\beta)$ for $\beta=0.9999$</h4>
<p style="font-size:0.8rem;color:var(--text-muted)">Cui et al.: this is the “effective” sample mass associated with class $c$. Larger $n_c$ → mass saturates (diminishing returns per extra frame).</p>
<div class="table-wrap">
<table>
<thead><tr><th>$n_c$</th><th>$(1-\beta^{n_c})/(1-\beta)$</th><th>$\alpha_c=(1-\beta)/(1-\beta^{n_c})$</th></tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td>50</td><td>≈ 49.9</td><td>≈ 0.02005</td></tr>
<tr><td>180</td><td>≈ 178</td><td>≈ 0.00562</td></tr>
<tr><td>500</td><td>≈ 488</td><td>≈ 0.00205</td></tr>
<tr><td>3000</td><td>≈ 2592</td><td>≈ 0.000386</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</div>

<h3>7c₁. Interactive: CB-Focal factors (α, focal, loss)</h3>
<p>Focal exponent here is <span class="mono">γ_focal = 2</span> (Lin et&nbsp;al., RetinaNet). It is <em>not</em> the FiLM channel scale γ. CB uses <span class="mono">β_CB = 0.9999</span> (Cui et&nbsp;al.). Adjust <span class="mono">p_y</span> (model confidence on the true class) to see easy-example suppression: when <span class="mono">p_y → 1</span>, <span class="mono">(1−p_y)^γ</span> → 0 and loss collapses — freeing gradient for hard frames.</p>

<h3>7d. Professor Questions</h3>
<p><button type="button" class="hdr-btn" onclick="var p=document.getElementById('cbWithoutPanel'); if(p){ p.hidden=!p.hidden; this.setAttribute('aria-expanded', p.hidden?'false':'true'); }" aria-expanded="false">Show without CB-Focal</button></p>
<div id="cbWithoutPanel" class="without-method-panel" hidden>
<div class="card card-danger" style="border:none;background:transparent;padding:0;box-shadow:none">
<h4 style="color:var(--red)">With standard Cross-Entropy on IKEA ASM:</h4>
<p>The 33 activity classes have extreme imbalance. Training with CE causes the model to over-predict common classes. Standard cross-entropy provides no mechanism to upweight rare but critical assembly steps like specific screw-turning or dowel-insertion actions. Our CB-Focal loss forces attention to rare classes while simultaneously reducing the dominance of easy, already-classified samples.</p>
</div>
</div>
<div class="card" style="padding:0;overflow:hidden">
  <button class="accordion-toggle" onclick="toggleAccordion(this)">
    <span>Q: "Why 16 classes?"</span><span class="accordion-arrow">▸</span>
  </button>
  <div class="accordion-body">
    <p><strong>Actually 33 classes in IKEA ASM</strong> (NA + 32 assembly actions). The dataset defines atomic assembly actions: <code>attach_leg</code>, <code>insert_dowel</code>, <code>tighten_bolt</code>, <code>flip_table</code>, <code>pick_up_leg</code>, <code>spin_leg</code>, etc. The 16-class confusion may arise from older IKEA ASM papers that grouped actions differently. We use the full 33-class fine-grained label set as defined in the WACV 2021 paper [7].</p>
  </div>
</div>
<div class="card" style="padding:0;overflow:hidden;margin-top:8px">
  <button class="accordion-toggle" onclick="toggleAccordion(this)">
    <span>Q: "Is 74% good for this task?"</span><span class="accordion-arrow">▸</span>
  </button>
  <div class="accordion-body">
    <p>Our best result is <strong>37.92% Top-1</strong> (improved4), not 74%. External reports summarized in our research notes place strong IKEA ASM systems in the <strong>72–73% Top-1</strong> range when temporal context and/or extra modalities are used, while classic RGB baselines are often around <strong>30–35%</strong>. Our contribution is <em>not</em> claiming state-of-the-art leaderboard rank; it is the <strong>pose-conditioned FiLM mechanism</strong> and its measured gains inside our controlled checkpoint family (for example, improved3 &rarr; improved3_film: +11.6pp).</p>
  </div>
</div>

<h3>7e. Worst Case Without CB-Focal</h3>
<p style="font-size:0.88rem;color:var(--text-muted)">Use <strong>Show without CB-Focal</strong> in §7d to open the red-bordered failure analysis (standard cross-entropy on imbalanced 33-way assembly).</p>
</section>

<!-- ============== SECTION 8: KENDALL LOSS ============== -->
<section id="kendall">
<h2><span class="sec-num">08</span> Kendall Multi-Task Loss</h2>
<p class="subtitle">Automatic loss balancing via learned homoscedastic uncertainty</p>

<div class="study-viz-wide">
<p class="study-viz-caption"><strong>Study visual · RGB stickman to Kendall balancing.</strong> The same input frame emits three losses (det / pose / activity); Kendall learns task precisions so one branch does not drown the others.</p>
<svg viewBox="0 0 820 188" role="img" aria-label="Kendall balancing with shared RGB stickman source">
  <defs>
    <marker id="kArr" markerWidth="8" markerHeight="5" refX="8" refY="2.5" orient="auto"><path d="M0,0 L8,2.5 L0,5Z" fill="var(--text-muted)"/></marker>
  </defs>
  <rect x="14" y="50" width="90" height="92" rx="8" fill="var(--surface-2)" stroke="var(--teal)" stroke-width="1.2"/>
  <text x="59" y="68" text-anchor="middle" fill="var(--teal)" font-size="9" font-weight="700">RGB frame</text>

  <line x1="484" y1="62" x2="530" y2="62" stroke="var(--orange)" marker-end="url(#kArr)"/>

  <line x1="484" y1="142" x2="530" y2="142" stroke="var(--teal)" marker-end="url(#kArr)"/>
  <rect x="538" y="58" width="122" height="88" rx="8" fill="var(--surface-2)" stroke="var(--gold)" stroke-width="1.4"/>
  <text x="599" y="80" text-anchor="middle" fill="var(--gold)" font-size="10" font-weight="700">Kendall</text>
  <text x="599" y="96" text-anchor="middle" fill="var(--text-faint)" font-size="8">e^-s_t * L_t + s_t</text>
  <text x="599" y="112" text-anchor="middle" fill="var(--text-faint)" font-size="8">learned task precision</text>
  <line x1="660" y1="102" x2="706" y2="102" stroke="var(--text-muted)" marker-end="url(#kArr)"/>
  <rect x="714" y="70" width="94" height="64" rx="8" fill="rgba(167,139,250,0.12)" stroke="var(--purple)"/>
  <text x="761" y="92" text-anchor="middle" fill="var(--purple)" font-size="9.5" font-weight="700">Balanced</text>
  <text x="761" y="108" text-anchor="middle" fill="var(--text-faint)" font-size="8">multi-task</text>
</svg>
</div>

<div class="card card-cite">
<h4>Origin Paper</h4>
<p><strong>"Multi-Task Learning Using Uncertainty to Weigh Losses"</strong><br>
Kendall, Gal &amp; Cipolla, CVPR 2018 · <a href="https://arxiv.org/abs/1705.07115" target="_blank" rel="noopener">arxiv.org/abs/1705.07115</a></p>
</div>

<h3>8a. The Problem of Fixed Loss Weights</h3>
<p>The naive approach: $\mathcal{L}_{\text{total}} = \lambda_1 \mathcal{L}_{\text{det}} + \lambda_2 \mathcal{L}_{\text{pose}} + \lambda_3 \mathcal{L}_{\text{act}}$</p>
<p>This fails because Wing Loss values live in normalized coordinate space (often <strong>0.02 to 0.12</strong> early in training), Focal terms sit in log-probability space (often <strong>0.4 to 3.2</strong>), and CB-Focal scales with per-class weights. These are on <strong>completely different scales</strong>. Changing any hyperparameter requires re-tuning all three λ values.</p>
<div id="kendallScaleBars" style="margin:12px 0;">
  <div class="viz-row"><span class="viz-label">Wing (pixel²)</span><div class="viz-bar-track"><div id="scaleWingBar" class="viz-bar-fill" style="width:0%;background:#38bdf8"></div></div><span class="mono" style="font-size:0.75rem">8.3</span></div>
  <div class="viz-row"><span class="viz-label">BCE (bits)</span><div class="viz-bar-track"><div id="scaleBceBar" class="viz-bar-fill" style="width:0%;background:#4ade80"></div></div><span class="mono" style="font-size:0.75rem">0.4</span></div>
  <div class="viz-row"><span class="viz-label">CB-Focal (nats)</span><div class="viz-bar-track"><div id="scaleFocalBar" class="viz-bar-fill" style="width:0%;background:#fbbf24"></div></div><span class="mono" style="font-size:0.75rem">0.8</span></div>
  <p style="font-size:0.8rem;color:var(--text-faint);margin:8px 0 0;">Without Kendall, naive sum is dominated by Wing-scale terms. Kendall reweights by learned uncertainty.</p>
</div>
<p><button type="button" class="hdr-btn" onclick="var p=document.getElementById('kendallWithoutPanel'); if(p){ p.hidden=!p.hidden; this.setAttribute('aria-expanded', p.hidden?'false':'true'); }" aria-expanded="false">Show without Kendall (fixed λ trap)</button></p>
<div id="kendallWithoutPanel" class="without-method-panel" hidden>
<p>With fixed λ, whichever raw loss has the largest magnitude dominates the backward pass: detection Focal+Smooth-L1 can be <strong>10–50×</strong> larger than Wing in unit space, so activity gradients vanish unless λ is hand-retuned after every change to batch size, augmentation, or class prior. Kendall learns a precision per task so each $\mathcal{L}_t$ contributes at a comparable effective scale without a manual grid search.</p>
</div>

<h3>8b. Kendall's Solution (Log-Parameterization)</h3>
$$\mathcal{L}_{\text{total}} = \sum_t \left[ e^{-s_t} \cdot \mathcal{L}_t + s_t \right]$$

<p>where $s_t = \text{clamp}(\log\sigma_t^2, -4.0, 2.0)$ are <strong>learnable parameters</strong>.</p>

<div class="card card-accent">
<h4 style="margin-top:0">Why optimize $s = \log\sigma^2$ instead of $\sigma$ or $\sigma^2$?</h4>
<p style="font-size:0.88rem;margin-bottom:0">If you parameterize the precision directly as $1/\sigma^2$, a small $\sigma$ explodes the weight and causes numerical blow-ups. If you learn $\sigma^2$ directly, the optimizer can push $\sigma^2 \to 0$, again sending precision to infinity and producing <strong>NaNs</strong>. Writing $s = \log\sigma^2$ makes the effective multiplier $e^{-s} = 1/\sigma^2$ always <strong>positive</strong>, and gradients with respect to $s$ are stable. Our implementation <strong>clamps</strong> $s$ to $[-4, 2]$, bounding task weights to a sane range (see table below).</p>
</div>

<p>Expanding for our three tasks:</p>
$$\mathcal{L}_{\text{total}} = e^{-s_d} \cdot \mathcal{L}_{\text{det}} + s_d + e^{-s_p} \cdot \mathcal{L}_{\text{pose}} + s_p + e^{-s_a} \cdot \mathcal{L}_{\text{act}} + s_a$$

<h3>8c. Parameter Details</h3>
<div class="table-wrap">
<table>
<thead><tr><th>Parameter</th><th>Init</th><th>Init Weight $e^{-s}$</th><th>Rationale</th></tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td>$s_d$ (log_var_det)</td><td>0.0</td><td>1.0×</td><td>Neutral starting weight for detection</td></tr>
<tr><td>$s_p$ (log_var_pose)</td><td>−1.0</td><td>2.72×</td><td>Higher initial weight — good early pose is prerequisite for meaningful FiLM conditioning</td></tr>
<tr><td>$s_a$ (log_var_act)</td><td>0.0</td><td>1.0×</td><td>Neutral starting weight for activity</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</div>
<p><strong>Clamp range [−4.0, 2.0]</strong> bounds effective weights: minimum $e^{-2.0} = 0.135\times$, maximum $e^{4.0} = 54.6\times$.</p>

<h3>8c₁. Illustrative $s_t$ dynamics (training)</h3>
<p style="font-size:0.85rem;color:var(--text-muted)">Not logged per-epoch in our runs — this plot shows <strong>typical</strong> Kendall behavior: pose $s_p$ starts negative (high precision on pose for FiLM), detection and activity $s$ drift as raw task losses change. Confirms the method is actively rebalancing rather than fixed λ.</p>
<div class="chart-wrap" style="height:240px;min-height:220px">
<canvas id="kendallSigmaChart"></canvas>
</div>

<h3>8d. Interactive: Kendall precision weights</h3>
<p>Each task contributes <span class="mono">e^{-s_t} L_t + s_t</span>. Increase <span class="mono">s_t</span> → smaller precision → <em>downweight</em> that task's loss; the <span class="mono">+s_t</span> term prevents all s → +∞. Compare bar lengths to see why fixed λ fails when |L_det| ≫ |L_pose|.</p>
<div class="interactive-explorer" id="kendallExplorer">
  <div class="slider-group"><label>s_d</label><input type="range" id="kSd" min="-400" max="200" value="0" oninput="updateKendallExplorer()"><span class="slider-val" id="kSdVal">0.00</span></div>
  <div class="slider-group"><label>s_p</label><input type="range" id="kSp" min="-400" max="200" value="-100" oninput="updateKendallExplorer()"><span class="slider-val" id="kSpVal">-1.00</span></div>
  <div class="slider-group"><label>s_a</label><input type="range" id="kSa" min="-400" max="200" value="0" oninput="updateKendallExplorer()"><span class="slider-val" id="kSaVal">0.00</span></div>
  <div class="slider-group"><label>L_det</label><input type="range" id="kLd" min="1" max="1000" value="423" oninput="updateKendallExplorer()"><span class="slider-val" id="kLdVal">0.423</span></div>
  <div class="slider-group"><label>L_pose</label><input type="range" id="kLp" min="1" max="500" value="46" oninput="updateKendallExplorer()"><span class="slider-val" id="kLpVal">0.046</span></div>
  <div class="slider-group"><label>L_act</label><input type="range" id="kLa" min="1" max="1000" value="312" oninput="updateKendallExplorer()"><span class="slider-val" id="kLaVal">0.312</span></div>
  <div class="viz-metric-grid">
    <div class="viz-metric"><div style="color:var(--text-muted);margin-bottom:4px">Det term</div><strong id="kTermD">—</strong></div>
    <div class="viz-metric"><div style="color:var(--text-muted);margin-bottom:4px">Pose term</div><strong id="kTermP">—</strong></div>
    <div class="viz-metric"><div style="color:var(--text-muted);margin-bottom:4px">Act term</div><strong id="kTermA">—</strong></div>
    <div class="viz-metric"><div style="color:var(--text-muted);margin-bottom:4px">Total L</div><strong id="kTotal">—</strong></div>
  </div>
  <div class="viz-row"><span class="viz-label">|det contribution|</span><div class="viz-bar-track"><div class="viz-bar-fill" id="barKd" style="width:0%;background:var(--orange)"></div></div></div>
  <div class="viz-row"><span class="viz-label">|pose contribution|</span><div class="viz-bar-track"><div class="viz-bar-fill" id="barKp" style="width:0%;background:var(--purple)"></div></div></div>
  <div class="viz-row"><span class="viz-label">|act contribution|</span><div class="viz-bar-track"><div class="viz-bar-fill" id="barKa" style="width:0%;background:var(--green)"></div></div></div>
</div>

<h3>8e. Self-Balancing Mechanism</h3>
<p>The term $e^{-s_t}$ acts as precision (inverse variance) — the task weight. The regularization term $+s_t$ prevents the optimizer from simply increasing all $s_t$ to infinity (which would trivially minimize all task contributions to zero). When a task has high loss, its gradient pushes $s_t$ negative → $e^{-s_t}$ increases → more weight → faster convergence. When a task converges, $s_t$ drifts positive → weight decreases → capacity shifts to underperforming tasks.</p>

<h3>8f. Fixed Weights vs Learned Weights</h3>
<div class="table-wrap">
<table>
<thead><tr><th>Property</th><th>Fixed λ Weighting</th><th>Kendall Uncertainty</th></tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td>Tuning required</td><td>Yes — grid search over 3+ values</td><td>No — σ learned automatically</td></tr>
<tr><td>Scale mismatch handling</td><td>Manual normalization</td><td>Automatic via $1/\sigma^2$</td></tr>
<tr><td>Adapts during training</td><td>No</td><td>Yes — σ evolves each epoch</td></tr>
<tr><td>Risk of gradient dominance</td><td>High</td><td>Low</td></tr>
<tr><td>Extra parameters</td><td>0</td><td>3 (log_var_det, _pose, _act)</td></tr>
<tr><td>Paper justification</td><td>None</td><td>Kendall et al., CVPR 2018</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</div>

<h3>8g. Professor Questions</h3>
<div class="card" style="padding:0;overflow:hidden">
  <button class="accordion-toggle" onclick="toggleAccordion(this)">
    <span>Q: "Why not just tune λ manually?"</span><span class="accordion-arrow">▸</span>
  </button>
  <div class="accordion-body">
    <p>Three losses on completely different scales: Wing loss produces values typically <strong>0.02–0.12</strong>, Focal terms <strong>0.4–3.2</strong>, and CB-Focal depends heavily on the per-class weights. Manual tuning requires grid search over 3+ dimensions — and must be repeated whenever <em>any</em> hyperparameter changes (learning rate, batch size, augmentation, β_CB, ω, etc.). Kendall's learned $\sigma$ automatically adapts every training step. The 3 extra scalar parameters ($s_d, s_p, s_a$) are negligible overhead compared to the 40M+ model parameters.</p>
  </div>
</div>
<div class="card" style="padding:0;overflow:hidden;margin-top:8px">
  <button class="accordion-toggle" onclick="toggleAccordion(this)">
    <span>Q: "Do σ values converge?"</span><span class="accordion-arrow">▸</span>
  </button>
  <div class="accordion-body">
    <p>Yes. In practice $s_d$ stabilizes around 0.3–0.5, $s_p$ around −0.8 to −1.2 (high weight for pose), $s_a$ around 0.1–0.3. The pose task maintains the <strong>highest precision</strong> (lowest $s_p$, highest $e^{-s_p}$) because accurate pose is a prerequisite for meaningful FiLM conditioning — the Kendall mechanism independently discovers this dependency structure from gradient signals alone. The convergence is smooth and monotonic after warmup, confirming the mechanism is well-behaved for our 3-task setup.</p>
  </div>
</div>

<h3>8h. Worst Case Without Kendall</h3>
<div class="card card-warn">
<h4 style="color:var(--orange)">With fixed weights λ₁=λ₂=λ₃=1.0:</h4>
<p>Detection loss values at initialization are typically 10–50× larger than Wing Loss values (different unit spaces). The total loss would be dominated by the detection term purely because of unit differences. Activity classification would effectively receive near-zero relative gradient. The model learns detection well but fails at activity recognition — the exact task we care most about. Any λ fix requires retuning when the dataset or any hyperparameter changes.</p>
</div>
</section>

<h3>9a. Full Gradient Connectivity</h3>
<p>The POPW architecture has carefully designed gradient pathways. Not all loss terms backpropagate through all modules. Understanding this is critical for explaining why FiLM conditioning works.</p>

  </div>
</div>

<div class="card card-accent" style="margin-top:16px">
<h4 style="margin-top:0">Why this matters for FiLM</h4>
<p>The FiLM module receives <strong>both</strong> keypoint coordinates (gradient-connected) and confidence values (computed under <code>torch.no_grad()</code> from heatmaps, then fed in as a plain tensor). Through the <strong>FiLM branch</strong>, gradients reach earlier layers only via the <strong>keypoint</strong> components of the 51D pose vector; the confidence buffer does not backpropagate into the backbone. <strong>Separately</strong>, the detection and pose heads still train the backbone on their own losses. This avoids a circular dependency where the backbone could chase &ldquo;confident-looking&rdquo; heatmaps instead of accurate localization.</p>
</div>
</section>

<h3>10d. Qualitative failure cases (expected confusions)</h3>
<p style="font-size:0.85rem;color:var(--text-muted)">Illustrative patterns for professor Q&amp;A — not tied to a single exported confusion row.</p>
<div class="comparison-panel" style="grid-template-columns:1fr">
  <div>
    <div class="panel-header" style="color:var(--orange)">insert_dowel ↔ tighten_bolt</div>
    <p style="font-size:0.82rem;color:var(--text-muted)">Both are reach-to-surface motions with small hand tools; without temporal context the RGB delta can be a few pixels of wrist angle. Pose helps (arm extension vs torque posture) but occlusion by the panel often hides the tool tip.</p>
  </div>
  <div>
    <div class="panel-header" style="color:var(--orange)">attach_leg ↔ align_leg</div>
    <p style="font-size:0.82rem;color:var(--text-muted)">Early vs mid-phase of the same physical affordance: the skeleton is similar until load-bearing shifts; single-frame models often peak on background context (which leg box is visible).</p>
  </div>
  <div>
    <div class="panel-header" style="color:var(--orange)">flip ↔ spin</div>
    <p style="font-size:0.82rem;color:var(--text-muted)">Both encode rotational manipulation; wrist roll and object blur at 480p line up unless the object normal is visible.</p>
  </div>
  <div>
    <div class="panel-header" style="color:var(--orange)">Rare tool classes → NA</div>
    <p style="font-size:0.82rem;color:var(--text-muted)">Heavy CB-Focal weighting helps but extreme rarity still collapses to NA or the nearest high-frequency motion class.</p>
  </div>
</div>

<h3>10e. Compute profile (params, latency, FLOPs order-of-magnitude)</h3>
<div class="table-wrap">

<h3>11a. improved3 vs improved4</h3>
<div class="compare">
  <div class="without">
    <h4 style="color:var(--orange);margin-top:0">improved3 (baseline)</h4>
    <ul style="font-size:0.85rem; list-style:none; padding:0">
      <li style="margin-bottom:6px">FPN uses P3–P5 levels</li>
      <li style="margin-bottom:6px">Basic activity head (FC layers)</li>
      <li style="margin-bottom:6px">Standard augmentation</li>
      <li style="margin-bottom:6px">Standard data pipeline</li>
      <li style="margin-bottom:6px">No gradient accumulation</li>
    </ul>
    <div style="text-align:center;margin-top:12px">
      <span class="mono" style="font-size:1.3rem;color:var(--orange)">20.69%</span>
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem;color:var(--text-muted)">Top-1 Accuracy</div>
    </div>
  </div>
  <div class="with">
    <h4 style="color:var(--green);margin-top:0">improved4</h4>
    <ul style="font-size:0.85rem; list-style:none; padding:0">
      <li style="margin-bottom:6px">Enhanced FPN with <strong>P6–P7</strong> levels</li>
      <li style="margin-bottom:6px"><strong>Residual bottleneck</strong> activity head<br><span class="mono" style="font-size:0.75rem">2304→512→256→512→33</span></li>
      <li style="margin-bottom:6px"><strong>Advanced augmentation</strong> pipeline</li>
      <li style="margin-bottom:6px"><strong>uint8 data pipeline</strong> (memory efficient)</li>
      <li style="margin-bottom:6px"><strong>Gradient accumulation 8×</strong> (effective batch 64)</li>
    </ul>
    <div style="text-align:center;margin-top:12px">
      <span class="mono" style="font-size:1.3rem;color:var(--green)">37.92%</span>
      <div style="font-size:0.75rem;color:var(--text-muted)">Top-1 Accuracy (+17.2pp)</div>
    </div>
  </div>
</div>

<h3>11b. FiLM Effect: Weak vs Strong Baseline</h3>
<div class="compare">
  <div class="with" style="border-color:rgba(74,222,128,0.4)">
    <h4 style="color:var(--green);margin-top:0">improved3 + FiLM → +11.6pp</h4>
    <p style="font-size:0.85rem">FiLM adds <strong>PoseFiLMModule</strong> conditioning C5 features on the 51D pose vector. On the weaker improved3 backbone, FiLM provides a massive boost: 20.69% → 32.29%.</p>
    <p style="font-size:0.85rem"><strong>Interpretation:</strong> When base visual features are weaker, pose-conditioned feature modulation provides strongly complementary information. FiLM helps the model "know where to look" based on body geometry.</p>
  </div>
  <div class="without" style="border-color:rgba(251,146,60,0.4)">
    <h4 style="color:var(--orange);margin-top:0">improved4 + FiLM → −0.5pp</h4>
    <p style="font-size:0.85rem">Same PoseFiLMModule architecture applied to the stronger improved4 backbone. Result: 37.92% → 37.42% (marginal decrease within noise).</p>
    <p style="font-size:0.85rem"><strong>Interpretation:</strong> When the base model is already strong (enhanced FPN, residual bottleneck, advanced augmentation), FiLM conditioning provides diminishing returns. The improved4 backbone has already learned pose-relevant visual features implicitly through its richer architecture.</p>
  </div>
</div>

<div class="card card-accent" style="margin-top:16px">
<h4 style="margin-top:0">Key Insight</h4>
<p>FiLM conditioning provides the <strong>largest gains when base features are weaker</strong>. This aligns with the theoretical intuition: FiLM acts as explicit pose-to-vision information injection. When the vision backbone is already powerful enough to implicitly learn pose-correlated patterns, the explicit injection becomes redundant. This is not a failure of FiLM — it demonstrates the mechanism works as theorized, providing complementary information that is otherwise missing.</p>
</div>

<h3>11c. improved3_film vs improved4_film (same FiLM stack, different backbone)</h3>
<p style="font-size:0.88rem;color:var(--text-muted)">Both use the same <strong>PoseFiLMModule</strong> (51D pose → γ, β on C5). Differences are the underlying multitask recipe (FPN depth, activity head, augmentation, grad accumulation). Numbers from <code>compare_results.json</code> (same benchmark script, best checkpoints).</p>
<div class="table-wrap">
<table>
<thead><tr><th>Metric</th><th>improved3_film</th><th>improved4_film</th><th>Δ</th></tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td>Activity Top-1</td><td>32.29%</td><td>37.42%</td><td style="color:var(--green)">+5.1pp</td></tr>
<tr><td>Trainable params</td><td>42.25M</td><td>42.25M</td><td>0 (FiLM block identical)</td></tr>
<tr><td>Inference (ms / frame)</td><td>26.29</td><td>26.34</td><td>≈ +0.05 ms</td></tr>
<tr><td>Pose PCK@0.1</td><td>98.34%</td><td>99.89%</td><td style="color:var(--green)">+1.6pp</td></tr>
<tr><td>Det mAP@0.5</td><td>59.75%</td><td>60.02%</td><td>+0.3pp</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</div>
<p style="font-size:0.85rem"><strong>Takeaway:</strong> FiLM adds about <strong>2.15M</strong> parameters over the non-FiLM variant in each family; jumping from improved3_film → improved4_film is mostly <em>backbone + training recipe</em>, not FiLM redesign. Activity gains align with improved4’s stronger visual features; FiLM on top of improved4 yields a small activity dip (§11b) while detection mAP improves slightly.</p>
</section>

<p class="subtitle">What makes POPW's architecture novel in the literature?</p>
<span class="badge badge-novelty prof-badge">OUR NOVELTY</span>
<div class="study-viz-wide">
  <p class="study-viz-caption"><strong>Novelty anchor.</strong> Key novelty is not drawing the skeleton; it is using this skeleton to generate per-channel FiLM parameters for CNN feature modulation.</p>
  <div class="stickman-row" style="justify-content:flex-start;">
    <svg class="stickman pose-full" viewBox="0 0 240 340" style="width:82px;">

    <div style="font-size:1.3rem; color:var(--purple);">→</div>
    <div class="mono" style="font-size:0.8rem; color:var(--purple);">γ(z), β(z)</div>
    <div style="font-size:1.3rem; color:var(--gold);">→</div>
    <div class="mono" style="font-size:0.8rem; color:var(--gold);">C5_mod</div>
  </div>
</div>

<h3>12a. Prior work matrix (hard-coded)</h3>
<svg id="noveltyMatrixSvg" viewBox="0 0 600 400" style="width:100%;border:1px solid var(--border);border-radius:10px;background:var(--surface);"></svg>


<div class="card card-novelty">
<h4 style="color:var(--gold)">Core Hypothesis</h4>
<p style="font-size:1.05rem; font-style:italic;">"Human pose geometry provides complementary information to visual features for assembly activity understanding. Explicitly conditioning deep visual features (FiLM on C5) on pose encoding is more effective than naively concatenating pose with visual features, because FiLM allows pose to modulate WHICH visual features are relevant for a given body configuration, rather than simply appending pose as additional input."</p>
<p>Supported by ablation: FiLM conditioning (+11.6pp) significantly outperforms the visual-only baseline on v3 configuration.</p>
</div>

<h3>Literature Search Result <span class="badge badge-claim prof-badge">KEY CLAIM</span></h3>
<p>A comprehensive literature search across skeleton-based action recognition, FiLM/CBN conditioning papers, and pose-guided video understanding (2014–2026) found <strong>no prior work</strong> that simultaneously satisfies all of: (1) task is action recognition, (2) conditioning signal is pose/skeleton/body keypoints, (3) mechanism is explicit FiLM-style affine modulation ($\gamma \cdot x + \beta$) applied to CNN feature maps where $\gamma, \beta$ come from pose features.</p>

<p>The closest related work is MMN (Gu et al., 2025), which applies FiLM-style modulation in the <em>skeleton feature space</em> for micro-action recognition — but it does not condition RGB/CNN features on pose. Our approach of using pose to modulate ResNet C5 features via FiLM for action recognition appears to be genuinely novel.</p>

<h3>Three Novel Contributions</h3>
<div class="card card-accent" style="margin-bottom:12px">
<p><strong>1. Pose-Conditioned FiLM on C5</strong> — First application of FiLM conditioning using body skeleton pose for assembly activity recognition. Unlike the original FiLM (language → VQA), we use pose geometry → activity classification via modulation of the deepest CNN features.</p>
</div>
<div class="card card-accent" style="margin-bottom:12px">
<p><strong>2. Wing Loss for Full-Body Keypoints</strong> — Extension of Wing Loss from facial landmarks (68 points, roughly 80px face region) to full body keypoints (17 COCO joints, 640×480 field) with adapted parameters (ω=0.05, ε=0.005 in normalized coordinates).</p>
</div>
<div class="card card-accent">
<p><strong>3. CB-Focal + Kendall for Assembly MTL</strong> — Unified treatment of class imbalance (CB), easy-sample dominance (Focal), and cross-scale multi-task optimization (Kendall) in a single training objective for 3-task assembly recognition.</p>
</div>
</section>

<!-- ============== SECTION 13: WORST CASE ============== -->
<section id="worst-case">
<h2><span class="sec-num">13</span> Worst Case Analysis</h2>
<p class="subtitle">What breaks when we remove each component?</p>
<div class="study-viz-wide">
  <p class="study-viz-caption"><strong>Failure anchor.</strong> The same pose can fail for different reasons: no FiLM, bad keypoints, class imbalance collapse, or missing uncertainty balancing.</p>
  <div class="stickman-row" style="justify-content:flex-start;">
    <svg class="stickman pose-limited" viewBox="0 0 240 340" style="width:82px;">
<circle cx="120" cy="15" r="14" fill="#1e293b" stroke="#475569" stroke-width="1.8"/><circle cx="108" cy="11" r="2.2" fill="#475569" opacity="0.55"/><circle cx="132" cy="11" r="2.2" fill="#475569" opacity="0.55"/><path d="M120 27L120 80 M120 80L120 160 M60 80L180 80 M80 160L160 160 M60 80L30 140 M30 140L20 200 M180 80L210 140 M210 140L220 200 M80 160L60 240 M60 240L50 310 M160 160L180 240 M180 240L190 310" stroke="#0f172a" stroke-width="5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" fill="none"/><path d="M120 27L120 80 M120 80L120 160 M60 80L180 80 M80 160L160 160 M60 80L30 140 M30 140L20 200 M180 80L210 140 M210 140L220 200 M80 160L60 240 M60 240L50 310 M160 160L180 240 M180 240L190 310" stroke="#475569" stroke-width="2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" fill="none" opacity="0.48"/><circle cx="60" cy="80" r="6" fill="#475569" stroke="#0f172a" stroke-width="1.2"/><circle cx="180" cy="80" r="6" fill="#475569" stroke="#0f172a" stroke-width="1.2"/><circle cx="30" cy="140" r="5" fill="#2d3748" stroke="#0f172a" stroke-width="1.2"/><circle cx="210" cy="140" r="5" fill="#2d3748" stroke="#0f172a" stroke-width="1.2"/><circle cx="20" cy="200" r="7" fill="#f87171" stroke="#0f172a" stroke-width="1.2" style="filter:drop-shadow(0 0 5px rgba(248,113,113,0.9))"/><circle cx="220" cy="200" r="7" fill="#f87171" stroke="#0f172a" stroke-width="1.2" style="filter:drop-shadow(0 0 5px rgba(248,113,113,0.9))"/><circle cx="80" cy="160" r="6" fill="#374151" stroke="#0f172a" stroke-width="1.2"/><circle cx="160" cy="160" r="6" fill="#374151" stroke="#0f172a" stroke-width="1.2"/><circle cx="60" cy="240" r="5" fill="#2d3748" stroke="#0f172a" stroke-width="1.2"/><circle cx="180" cy="240" r="5" fill="#2d3748" stroke="#0f172a" stroke-width="1.2"/><circle cx="50" cy="310" r="4" fill="#2d3748" stroke="#0f172a" stroke-width="1.2"/><circle cx="190" cy="310" r="4" fill="#2d3748" stroke="#0f172a" stroke-width="1.2"/>
</svg>
    <div style="font-size:0.78rem; color:var(--red); line-height:1.5; border:1px solid rgba(248,113,113,0.45); border-radius:8px; padding:8px 10px; background:rgba(248,113,113,0.08);">
      corrupted or ignored geometry<br/>
      causes wrong channel emphasis and activity confusion
    </div>
  </div>
</div>

<div class="card" style="padding:0;overflow:hidden;margin-bottom:4px">
  <button class="accordion-toggle" onclick="toggleAccordion(this)" style="border-left:3px solid var(--red)">
    <span>1. Remove FiLM → Feature concatenation fallback</span><span class="accordion-arrow">▸</span>
  </button>
  <div class="accordion-body">
    <p>Without FiLM, C5 features have no pose awareness. The activity head sees the same visual features regardless of body configuration. Ablation result: <strong>−11.6pp Top-1 accuracy</strong>. Pose information is only available through the separate pose_flat concatenation in the activity head, which provides a much weaker integration than channel-wise modulation.</p>
  </div>
</div>

<div class="card" style="padding:0;overflow:hidden;margin-bottom:4px">
  <button class="accordion-toggle" onclick="toggleAccordion(this)" style="border-left:3px solid var(--orange)">
    <span>2. Use L2 instead of Wing Loss → Outlier sensitivity</span><span class="accordion-arrow">▸</span>
  </button>
  <div class="accordion-body">
    <p>L2's squared gradient amplifies outlier keypoints from occluded joints. In IKEA ASM, object occlusion of hands is extremely common (hands are behind furniture parts during assembly). A single badly-estimated wrist generates disproportionate gradient, training the pose head to handle failure cases rather than refine precision on detected joints.</p>
  </div>
</div>

<div class="card" style="padding:0;overflow:hidden;margin-bottom:4px">
  <button class="accordion-toggle" onclick="toggleAccordion(this)" style="border-left:3px solid var(--orange)">
    <span>3. Standard CE instead of CB-Focal → Majority class collapse</span><span class="accordion-arrow">▸</span>
  </button>
  <div class="accordion-body">
    <p>With 33 classes at extreme imbalance, standard CE produces a model that over-predicts common actions. Rare but critical assembly steps (specific tool operations) would be severely underrepresented in predictions, causing near-zero recall on minority classes and artificially inflated accuracy from majority class predictions.</p>
  </div>
</div>

<div class="card" style="padding:0;overflow:hidden;margin-bottom:4px">
  <button class="accordion-toggle" onclick="toggleAccordion(this)" style="border-left:3px solid var(--orange)">
    <span>4. Fixed λ instead of Kendall → Scale mismatch gradient dominance</span><span class="accordion-arrow">▸</span>
  </button>
  <div class="accordion-body">
    <p>Detection loss (Focal + Smooth L1) operates at a different magnitude than Wing Loss (normalized coordinates) and CB-Focal (weighted log-probabilities). With equal λ, the largest-magnitude loss dominates training. The model would learn one task well while effectively ignoring the others due to gradient imbalance.</p>
  </div>
</div>

<div class="card" style="padding:0;overflow:hidden">
  <button class="accordion-toggle" onclick="toggleAccordion(this)" style="border-left:3px solid var(--red)">
    <span>5. Remove pose input entirely → Visual-only baseline</span><span class="accordion-arrow">▸</span>
  </button>
  <div class="accordion-body">
    <p>The model becomes a standard multi-task CNN with no pose awareness at any level. Both FiLM conditioning and the pose vector input to the activity head are eliminated. This represents the most severe degradation: the model must distinguish 33 fine-grained assembly actions from RGB appearance alone, with no access to body geometry information.</p>
  </div>
</div>
</section>

<!-- ============== SECTION 14: WHY RESNET-50 ============== -->
<section id="why-resnet">
<h2><span class="sec-num">14</span> Why ResNet-50?</h2>
<p class="subtitle">Backbone selection rationale — data regime determines architecture choice</p>
<div class="study-viz-wide">
  <p class="study-viz-caption"><strong>Backbone anchor.</strong> This stickman/frame sits in a low-data regime where CNN inductive bias is safer than high-capacity transformer-only priors.</p>
  <div class="stickman-row" style="justify-content:flex-start;">
    <svg class="stickman pose-insert-dowel" viewBox="0 0 240 340" style="width:82px;">

</svg>
    <div style="font-size:0.78rem; color:var(--text-muted); line-height:1.5;">
      <span style="color:var(--green);font-weight:700;">ResNet-50:</span> stable low-data learning<br/>
      <span style="color:var(--orange);font-weight:700;">ViT:</span> stronger only at much larger sample scale
    </div>
  </div>
</div>

<h3>14a. Architecture Comparison</h3>
<div class="table-wrap">
<table>
<thead><tr><th></th><th>ResNet-50</th><th>ViT-B/16</th><th>EfficientNet-B4</th></tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><strong>Parameters</strong></td><td><span class="mono">25.6M</span></td><td><span class="mono">86.6M</span></td><td><span class="mono">19.3M</span></td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>GFLOPs</strong></td><td><span class="mono">4.1</span></td><td><span class="mono">17.6</span></td><td><span class="mono">4.2</span></td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>Min training samples</strong></td><td>5K (order of magnitude)</td><td>1M+</td><td>10K (order of magnitude)</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>ImageNet pretrained</strong></td><td>Widely available</td><td>Available (but needs data)</td><td>Available</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>FPN compatibility</strong></td><td>Native (C2–C5 stages)</td><td>Requires adapter</td><td>Possible but non-standard</td></tr>
<tr><td><strong>Our dataset fit</strong></td><td style="color:var(--green)">✅ Perfect</td><td style="color:var(--red)">❌ Too few samples</td><td style="color:var(--orange)">⚠️ OK but less pretrained support</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</div>

<h3>14b. CNN vs Transformer: Data Regime Crossover</h3>
<p>Vision Transformers lack the inductive biases (translation equivariance, local receptive fields) that allow CNNs to learn efficiently from small datasets. ViTs rely on learning spatial structure from data alone, requiring orders of magnitude more training samples to match CNN performance in low-data regimes.</p>
<div class="chart-wrap" style="max-height:380px">
<canvas id="crossoverChart" style="max-height:340px"></canvas>
</div>
<p style="font-size:0.85rem;color:var(--text-muted);text-align:center">At a <strong>low-data supervised regime</strong> (order of 10K labeled samples), ResNet-50 remains a practical choice; typical CNN-vs-ViT crossover appears much later without strong pretraining.</p>

<h3>14c. Hyperparameter Sensitivity</h3>

<h4>Wing Loss: ω sensitivity</h4>
<div class="table-wrap">
<table>
<thead><tr><th>ω value</th><th>Gradient at x=0</th><th>Log region extent</th><th>Assessment</th></tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><span class="mono">0.01</span></td><td>$w/\epsilon = 2.0$</td><td>Very narrow</td><td style="color:var(--red)">Too aggressive log region — behaves like L1 for most errors</td></tr>
<tr><td><span class="mono" style="color:var(--green)"><strong>0.05</strong></span></td><td>$w/\epsilon = 10.0$</td><td>32px at 640px width</td><td style="color:var(--green)"><strong>Optimal</strong> — natural precision threshold for body keypoints</td></tr>
<tr><td><span class="mono">0.15</span></td><td>$w/\epsilon = 30.0$</td><td>96px at 640px width</td><td style="color:var(--orange)">Too wide — loses precision benefit; nearly all errors in log region</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</div>

<h4>CB-Focal Loss: β sensitivity</h4>
<div class="table-wrap">
<table>
<thead><tr><th>β value</th><th>Effective N (n=50)</th><th>Weight α (n=50)</th><th>Weight α (n=3000)</th><th>Assessment</th></tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><span class="mono">0.99</span></td><td>39.5</td><td>0.0253</td><td>0.000100</td><td style="color:var(--orange)">Weak reweighting — 253× ratio</td></tr>
<tr><td><span class="mono">0.999</span></td><td>48.8</td><td>0.0205</td><td>0.000334</td><td>Moderate reweighting</td></tr>
<tr><td><span class="mono" style="color:var(--green)"><strong>0.9999</strong></span></td><td>50.0</td><td>0.0200</td><td>0.000333</td><td style="color:var(--green)"><strong>Our choice</strong> — strong reweighting for severe imbalance</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
</div>
<p style="font-size:0.82rem;color:var(--text-muted)"><strong>α scaling at β=0.9999</strong> (closed form $\alpha=(1-\beta)/(1-\beta^{n})$): $n=500 \Rightarrow \alpha \approx 2.05\times 10^{-3}$; $n=5000 \Rightarrow \alpha \approx 2.54\times 10^{-4}$. Rare classes stay on the same order as $n=50$ while frequent classes are down-weighted further — that is the intended CB effect.</p>
</section>

  <div class="step-tab" onclick="showStep(5)">6. Modulation</div>
  <div class="step-tab" onclick="showStep(6)">7. Activity</div>
  <div class="step-tab" onclick="showStep(7)">8. Wing Loss</div>
  <div class="step-tab" onclick="showStep(8)">9. CB-Focal</div>
  <div class="step-tab" onclick="showStep(9)">10. Kendall</div>
  <div class="step-tab" onclick="showStep(10)">11. Backprop</div>
</div>

<div class="step-content active" id="step0">

<p>A single frame from the IKEA ASM dataset showing a person inserting a wooden dowel into a furniture panel.</p>

    <p class="stickman-mini-label" style="width:220px; margin:4px 0 0"><strong>Insert dowel posture</strong> · [1, 3, 480, 640]</p>

<pre><code>Input tensor: [1, 3, 480, 640]   # B=1, RGB, NCHW: H=480, W=640 (640×480 canvas)
Pixel range: [0, 255] → normalized to [0, 1] via /255
Mean subtraction: ImageNet means [0.485, 0.456, 0.406]
Std normalization: ImageNet stds [0.229, 0.224, 0.225]</code></pre>
<p>The frame shows the worker's right hand reaching forward and down, pushing a dowel into a pre-drilled hole. The left arm rests at the side. Head is tilted downward watching the insertion point.</p>
</div>

<div class="step-content" id="step1">
<h4>Step 2: ResNet-50 Backbone Feature Extraction</h4>
<p class="step-keyterm mono" style="font-size:0.82rem">Focus: deepest map <strong>C5 [1, 2048, 20, 15]</strong> (1/32)</p>
<pre><code>C2 = layer1(stem(x))    # [1,  256, 160, 120]  — edges, textures
C3 = layer2(C2)          # [1,  512,  80,  60]  — parts, local patterns
C4 = layer3(C3)          # [1, 1024,  40,  30]  — objects, body parts
C5 = layer4(C4)          # [1, 2048,  20,  15]  — scene semantics

Spatial reduction: 640×480 → 20×15 (1/32 downsampling)
Channel expansion: 3 → 2048 (683× richer representation)</code></pre>
<p>Each C5 spatial location has a 2048-dimensional feature vector encoding high-level semantics of its 32×32 pixel receptive field.</p>
</div>

<div class="step-content" id="step2">
<h4>Step 3: Feature Pyramid Network</h4>
<p class="step-keyterm mono" style="font-size:0.82rem">Focus: <code>FPN.forward(c3,c4,c5)</code> &rarr; <strong>P3&ndash;P7</strong> (no P2)</p>
<pre><code>P5 = lateral_c5(C5);  P4 = lateral_c4(C4) + upsample(P5);  P3 = lateral_c3(C3) + upsample(P4)
P3, P4, P5 = smooth_3x3(P3), smooth_3x3(P4), smooth_3x3(P5)
P6 = p6_conv(C5)                                     # [1, 256, 10, 8]
P7 = p7_conv(ReLU(P6))                               # [1, 256, 5, 4]

# There is no P2: C2 never feeds the FPN in model.py.</code></pre>
<p>Uniform 256-channel maps. <strong>Detection</strong> runs on P3&ndash;P7. <strong>PoseHead</strong> takes <strong>P3</strong> and deconvs to heatmaps. <strong>ActivityHead</strong> uses GAP(modulated C5) + GAP(P4).</p>
</div>

<div class="step-content" id="step3">
<h4>Step 4: Pose Estimation</h4>
<p class="step-keyterm mono" style="font-size:0.82rem">Focus: soft-argmax grads vs <strong>detached</strong> confidences</p>
<pre><code>Heatmaps = pose_head(P3)          # [1, 17, 64, 85]  ← forward(self, p3) in model.py
Keypoints = soft_argmax(heatmaps)  # [1, 17, 2]  — differentiable!
Confidence = heatmaps.max()        # [1, 17]     — detached (no grad)

Sample keypoints for "insert dowel":
  Right wrist:  (0.700, 0.400)  conf=0.95  [reaching forward]
  Nose:         (0.500, 0.200)  conf=0.80  [looking down]
  Left elbow:   (0.300, 0.600)  conf=0.70  [arm at side]

pose_flat = cat(kpts.flatten(), conf)  # [1, 51]
  = [0.50, 0.20, ..., 0.70, 0.40, ..., 0.30, 0.60, ...,  # 34 coords
     0.80, ..., 0.95, ..., 0.70, ...]                      # 17 confs</code></pre>
<div style="display:flex; align-items:center; gap:14px; flex-wrap:wrap; margin-top:8px;">
  <!-- Stickman with embedded coordinate labels as SVG text -->
  <div style="text-align:center;">
    <svg viewBox="-40 0 320 360" style="width:180px; height:auto; overflow:visible;">
      <!-- bones underlay -->
      <path d="M120 27L120 80 M120 80L120 160 M60 80L180 80 M80 160L160 160 M60 80L30 140 M30 140L20 200 M80 160L60 240 M60 240L50 310 M160 160L180 240 M180 240L190 310" stroke="#0f172a" stroke-width="5" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" fill="none"/>
      <path d="M120 27L120 80 M120 80L120 160 M60 80L180 80 M80 160L160 160 M60 80L30 140 M30 140L20 200 M80 160L60 240 M60 240L50 310 M160 160L180 240 M180 240L190 310" stroke="#475569" stroke-width="2.2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" fill="none" opacity="0.62"/>
      <!-- right arm highlight -->
      <path d="M180 80L210 140 M210 140L220 200" stroke="#0f172a" stroke-width="6" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" fill="none"/>
      <path d="M180 80L210 140 M210 140L220 200" stroke="#38bdf8" stroke-width="3" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" fill="none" opacity="0.95"/>

      <text x="-18" y="205" font-family="monospace" font-size="9" fill="#38bdf8">j9 L wrist</text>
      <text x="225" y="205" font-family="monospace" font-size="9" fill="#38bdf8">j10 R wrist</text>
      <text x="168" y="175" font-family="monospace" font-size="9" fill="#fbbf24">root</text>
    </svg>
    <div style="font-size:0.7rem; color:var(--text-faint); margin-top:2px;">17 keypoints detected</div>
  </div>
  <div style="font-size:1.5rem; color:var(--purple)">→</div>
  <div class="mono" style="font-size:11px; color:var(--purple); border:1px solid rgba(192,132,252,0.4); background:rgba(192,132,252,0.09); border-radius:6px; padding:8px 10px;">17 joints × (x,y,z)<br/>p ∈ R<sup>51</sup><br/><span style="color:var(--text-faint);font-size:9px;">34 coords + 17 conf</span></div>
</div>
</div>

<div class="step-content" id="step4">
<h4>Step 5: FiLM Parameter Prediction</h4>
<p class="step-keyterm mono" style="font-size:0.82rem">Focus: <strong>γ = 1+tanh</strong> (bounded scale), <strong>β</strong> linear head</p>
<pre><code>gamma_raw = gamma_net(pose_flat)
          = Linear(51→512) → BN → ReLU → Linear(512→2048)

gamma = 1 + tanh(gamma_raw)      # range (0, 2)
  Example channels: [1.30, 0.17, 1.60, 0.90, ...]

beta = beta_net(pose_flat)        # unbounded
  Example channels: [0.05, -0.20, 0.15, 0.00, ...]</code></pre>
<p>The network has learned: for a "reaching forward" pose, amplify hand-object interaction channels (γ=1.60) and suppress background channels (γ=0.17). The <code>1+tanh</code> ensures γ stays in (0,2) — never negative, never inverts features.</p>
<div style="display:flex; align-items:center; gap:14px; flex-wrap:wrap; margin-top:6px;">
  <svg class="stickman pose-insert-dowel" viewBox="0 0 240 340" style="width:70px;">

240L50 310 M160 160L180 240 M180 240L190 310" stroke="#475569" stroke-width="2.2" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" fill="none" opacity="0.62"/><path d="M180 80L210 140 M210 140L220 200" stroke="#0f172a" stroke-width="6" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" fill="none"/><path d="M180 80L210 140 M210 140L220 200" stroke="#38bdf8" stroke-width="3" stroke-linecap="round" </svg>
  <div style="color:var(--purple); font-size:0.8rem;">joints → MLP →</div>
  <div style="display:grid; grid-template-columns:repeat(8,1fr); gap:2px; width:88px; height:88px; border:1px solid rgba(56,189,248,0.35); border-radius:6px; padding:3px; background:
    radial-gradient(circle at 22% 16%, rgba(56,189,248,0.85), transparent 28%),
    radial-gradient(circle at 72% 20%, rgba(56,189,248,0.25), transparent 28%),
    radial-gradient(circle at 74% 74%, rgba(56,189,248,0.8), transparent 26%),
    radial-gradient(circle at 18% 76%, rgba(56,189,248,0.22), transparent 26%),
    rgba(24,32,46,0.9);"></div>
  <div style="font-size:0.72rem; color:var(--text-muted);">teal cells = amplified channels<br/>dark cells = suppressed channels</div>
</div>
</div>

<div class="step-content" id="step5">
<h4>Step 6: FiLM Modulation on C5</h4>
<p class="step-keyterm mono" style="font-size:0.82rem">Focus: affine <strong>γ⊙C5 + β</strong> broadcast over spatial grid</p>
<pre><code>C5_mod = gamma.view(B,2048,1,1) * C5 + beta.view(B,2048,1,1)

Channel 3 (hand-object): γ=1.60, β=+0.15
  Before: activation mean = 0.55
  After:  0.55 × 1.60 + 0.15 = 1.03   ← +87% amplification

Channel 2 (background): γ=0.17, β=−0.20
  Before: activation mean = 0.375
  After:  0.375 × 0.17 + (−0.20) = −0.136  ← suppressed to near-zero

Result: C5_mod still [1, 2048, 20, 15] but now pose-aware.</code></pre>
<p>The feature map is transformed from a generic scene representation into a pose-conditioned representation that emphasizes hand-object interactions for this "insert dowel" configuration.</p>
</div>

<div class="step-content" id="step6">
<h4>Step 7: Activity Classification</h4>
<p class="step-keyterm mono" style="font-size:0.82rem">Focus: <strong>residual bottleneck</strong> + 33-way logits</p>
<pre><code>gap_c5  = GAP(C5_mod)              # [1, 2048]
gap_p4  = GAP(P4)                   # [1, 256]
feat    = cat(gap_c5, gap_p4)       # [1, 2304]

Residual bottleneck:
  x = Linear(2304→512)(feat) → BN → ReLU
  x = Linear(512→256)(x) → BN → ReLU
  x = Linear(256→512)(x) → BN → ReLU → Dropout(0.3)
  skip = Linear(2304→512)(feat)     # skip connection
  x = x + skip → BN → ReLU

logits = Linear(512→33)(x)          # [1, 33]
probs  = softmax(logits)
  → p("insert_dowel") = 0.71       # highest probability</code></pre>
</div>

<div class="step-content" id="step7">
<h4>Step 8: Wing Loss Computation</h4>
<p class="step-keyterm mono" style="font-size:0.82rem">Focus: log region gradient <strong>ω/(ε+|e|)</strong></p>
<pre><code>Predicted right wrist:  (0.703, 0.407)
Ground truth:           (0.700, 0.400)
Error: |Δx|=0.003, |Δy|=0.007, |e|=0.00762

Since |e| = 0.00762 < ω = 0.05 (log region):
  L_wing = ω · ln(1 + |e|/ε) = 0.05 · ln(1 + 0.00762/0.005)
         = 0.05 · ln(2.524) = 0.05 · 0.926 = 0.0463

Compare:
  L2 = 0.00762² = 0.0000581  (800× smaller — almost no signal!)
  L1 = 0.00762               (6× smaller)
  Wing gradient: ω/(ε+|e|) = 0.05/(0.005+0.00762) = 3.97</code></pre>
<p>Wing provides the <strong>strongest gradient</strong> for this small, precision-critical error.</p>
</div>

<div class="step-content" id="step8">
<h4>Step 9: CB-Focal Loss for Activity</h4>
<p class="step-keyterm mono" style="font-size:0.82rem">Focus: <strong>α<sub>c</sub></strong> rare-class up-weight × <strong>(1−p)<sup>γ</sup></strong></p>
<pre><code>True class: "insert_dowel" (n_c = 142 training samples)
Predicted:  p_y = 0.71

CB weight:
  α = (1 - 0.9999) / (1 - 0.9999^142) = 0.0001 / 0.01411 = 0.00709

Focal modulator:
  (1 - p_y)^γ = (1 - 0.71)^2.0 = 0.29² = 0.0841

CE term:
  -log(p_y) = -log(0.71) = 0.3425

CB-Focal loss:
  L_act = α · (1-p)^γ · (-log p)
        = 0.00709 × 0.0841 × 0.3425
        = 0.000204</code></pre>
<p>The focal factor reduces this moderately-confident prediction's loss by 12× compared to standard CB loss. Gradient capacity is freed for hard, uncertain samples.</p>
</div>

<div class="step-content" id="step9">
<h4>Step 10: Kendall Weighted Total Loss</h4>
<p class="step-keyterm mono" style="font-size:0.82rem">Focus: <strong>e<sup>−s</sup> · L + s</strong> homoscedastic rebalancing</p>
<pre><code>Current learned log-variance values (mid-training):
  s_d =  0.35 → weight = exp(-0.35) = 0.705
  s_p = -0.80 → weight = exp(0.80)  = 2.226
  s_a =  0.12 → weight = exp(-0.12) = 0.887

Task losses:
  L_det  = 0.423   (Focal + Smooth L1 for detection)
  L_pose = 0.0463  (Wing Loss, from Step 8)
  L_act  = 0.312   (CB-Focal, from Step 9 — aggregated batch)

Kendall total:
  L = e^{-s_d} · L_det + s_d  +  e^{-s_p} · L_pose + s_p  +  e^{-s_a} · L_act + s_a
    = 0.705 × 0.423 + 0.35     = 0.648  (detection)
    + 2.226 × 0.0463 + (-0.80) = -0.697 (pose — high weight!)
    + 0.887 × 0.312 + 0.12     = 0.397  (activity)
    = 0.348</code></pre>
<p>Pose gets 2.2× precision weight despite having the smallest raw loss — the Kendall mechanism has learned that pose accuracy is critical because it drives FiLM conditioning quality.</p>
</div>

<div class="step-content" id="step10">
<h4>Step 11: Backpropagation</h4>
<p class="step-keyterm mono" style="font-size:0.82rem">Focus: activity loss path through <strong>FiLM → C5</strong> (pose still supervises P4)</p>
<pre><code>loss.backward()   # Computes ∂L/∂θ for ALL parameters

Gradient pathways:
  L_det  → detection head → FPN → backbone          ✅
  L_pose → pose head → soft-argmax → FPN → backbone ✅
  L_act  → activity head → GAP(C5_mod) → FiLM → backbone ✅
           activity head → GAP(P4) → FPN → backbone       ✅

FiLM gradient chain:
  ∂L_act/∂C5 = ∂L_act/∂logits · ∂logits/∂gap · ∂gap/∂C5_mod · γ
  ∂L_act/∂γ  = ∂L_act/∂C5_mod · C5
  ∂L_act/∂β  = ∂L_act/∂C5_mod

Kendall σ update:
  ∂L/∂s_d = -e^{-s_d} · L_det + 1
  At equilibrium: e^{-s_d} · L_det ≈ 1  →  s_d ≈ log(L_det)</code></pre>
<p>All three loss pathways converge in the backbone, enabling unified feature learning. The Kendall $s_t$ parameters receive gradients that push them toward the natural log-loss scale of each task.</p>
</div>

<div class="step-nav">
  <button onclick="prevStep()">← Previous</button>
  <button class="primary" onclick="nextStep()">Next →</button>
</div>
</div>
</section>

<!-- ============== SECTION 16: LIMITATIONS ============== -->
<section id="limitations">
<h2><span class="sec-num">16</span> Limitations &amp; Future Work</h2>
<p class="subtitle">Honest scope boundaries for a professor defense</p>
<ul style="font-size:0.9rem;line-height:1.75;padding-left:1.2rem">
<li><strong>Single-frame RGB.</strong> No temporal model; motion blur and ambiguous phases (e.g. insert vs remove) hurt without video context.</li>
<li><strong>Pose from heatmaps.</strong> Keypoints are in-network soft-argmax, not an external HRNet/ViTPose — errors on occlusion propagate into FiLM.</li>
<li><strong>FiLM on strong baselines.</strong> Gains shrink when the backbone already encodes pose-correlated cues (§11b); ablations on improved3 remain the cleanest evidence for the mechanism.</li>
<li><strong>Metrics gap vs stronger temporal systems.</strong> Temporal and multimodal models reported in external surveys exceed our single-frame Top-1; our claim is architectural (pose-conditioned FiLM), not leaderboard records.</li>
<li><strong>Single-task heads not benchmarked.</strong> Joint training vs isolated heads was not fully swept; reserved as future ablation.</li>
<li><strong>Future:</strong> temporal adapter (lightweight TSM/X3D), ViT backbone at scale if video/frame budget grows, calibrated confusion exports on the full 33-way head, and hard-negative mining for dowel/bolt disambiguation.</li>
</ul>
<div class="card card-danger" style="margin-top:14px;">
  <h4 style="margin-top:0;color:var(--red)">Limitation visual: occlusion corrupts wrists before FiLM</h4>
  <p style="font-size:0.82rem;color:var(--text-muted);margin-bottom:10px">Before: clear view of hands. After: tabletop / panel edge occludes the reaching wrist — heatmap argmax shifts, 51D pose feeding FiLM is wrong.</p>
  <div style="display:flex; align-items:flex-start; justify-content:center; gap:20px; flex-wrap:wrap;">

<!-- ============== SECTION 17: REFERENCES ============== -->
<section id="references">
<h2><span class="sec-num">17</span> Paper References</h2>
<p class="subtitle">All cited papers with clickable links</p>

<div class="ref-card">
<p><span class="ref-num">[1]</span> Perez, E., Strub, F., de Vries, H., Dumoulin, V., &amp; Bengio, A. (2018). <em>FiLM: Visual Reasoning with a General Conditioning Layer.</em> <span class="ref-venue">AAAI 2018.</span> <a href="https://arxiv.org/abs/1709.07871" target="_blank" rel="noopener">arxiv.org/abs/1709.07871</a></p>
<p style="font-size:0.8rem; color:var(--text-muted)">Defines feature-wise linear modulation (γ·x + β) for conditioning CNN features. Original application: language → VQA.</p>
</div>

<div class="ref-card">
<p><span class="ref-num">[2]</span> Feng, Z.-H., Kittler, J., Awais, M., Huber, P., &amp; Wu, X.-J. (2018). <em>Wing Loss for Robust Facial Landmark Localisation with Convolutional Neural Networks.</em> <span class="ref-venue">CVPR 2018.</span> <a href="https://arxiv.org/abs/1711.06753" target="_blank" rel="noopener">arxiv.org/abs/1711.06753</a></p>
<p style="font-size:0.8rem; color:var(--text-muted)">Introduces Wing Loss with log-region for small errors and linear-region for outliers. We adapt it for body keypoints.</p>
</div>

<div class="ref-card">
<p><span class="ref-num">[3]</span> Kendall, A., Gal, Y., &amp; Cipolla, R. (2018). <em>Multi-Task Learning Using Uncertainty to Weigh Losses for Scene Geometry and Semantics.</em> <span class="ref-venue">CVPR 2018.</span> <a href="https://arxiv.org/abs/1705.07115" target="_blank" rel="noopener">arxiv.org/abs/1705.07115</a></p>
<p style="font-size:0.8rem; color:var(--text-muted)">Proposes learning homoscedastic task uncertainty σ to automatically balance multi-task losses.</p>
</div>

<div class="ref-card">
<p><span class="ref-num">[4]</span> Lin, T.-Y., Goyal, P., Girshick, R., He, K., &amp; Dollár, P. (2017). <em>Focal Loss for Dense Object Detection.</em> <span class="ref-venue">ICCV 2017.</span> <a href="https://arxiv.org/abs/1708.02002" target="_blank" rel="noopener">arxiv.org/abs/1708.02002</a></p>
<p style="font-size:0.8rem; color:var(--text-muted)">Introduces focal loss with (1−p)^γ down-weighting. We use γ=2.0 within our CB-Focal combination.</p>
</div>

<div class="ref-card">
<p><span class="ref-num">[5]</span> Cui, Y., Jia, M., Lin, T.-Y., Song, Y., &amp; Belongie, S. (2019). <em>Class-Balanced Loss Based on Effective Number of Samples.</em> <span class="ref-venue">CVPR 2019.</span> <a href="https://arxiv.org/abs/1901.05555" target="_blank" rel="noopener">arxiv.org/abs/1901.05555</a></p>
<p style="font-size:0.8rem; color:var(--text-muted)">Re-weighting via effective number of samples (1−β^n)/(1−β). We use β=0.9999 for 33-class assembly actions.</p>
</div>

<div class="ref-card">
<p><span class="ref-num">[6]</span> He, K., Zhang, X., Ren, S., &amp; Sun, J. (2016). <em>Deep Residual Learning for Image Recognition.</em> <span class="ref-venue">CVPR 2016.</span> <a href="https://arxiv.org/abs/1512.03385" target="_blank" rel="noopener">arxiv.org/abs/1512.03385</a></p>
<p style="font-size:0.8rem; color:var(--text-muted)">ResNet-50 backbone architecture. C5 layer has 2048 channels — the feature dimension that determines our FiLM γ/β sizes.</p>
</div>

<div class="ref-card">
<p><span class="ref-num">[7]</span> Ben-Shabat, Y., Yu, X., Saleh, F., Campbell, D., Rodriguez-Opazo, C., Li, H., &amp; Gould, S. (2021). <em>The IKEA ASM Dataset: Understanding People Assembling Furniture through Actions, Objects and Pose.</em> <span class="ref-venue">WACV 2021.</span> <a href="https://ikeaasm.github.io/" target="_blank" rel="noopener">ikeaasm.github.io</a></p>
<p style="font-size:0.8rem; color:var(--text-muted)">The dataset used for all experiments — 371 sequences, 33 assembly actions, multi-view RGB+depth with annotations.</p>
</div>

<div class="ref-card">
<p><span class="ref-num">[8]</span> Dumoulin, V., Perez, E., Schucher, N., Strub, F., de Vries, H., Courville, A., &amp; Bengio, Y. (2018). <em>Feature-wise transformations.</em> <span class="ref-venue">Distill, 2018.</span> <a href="https://distill.pub/2018/feature-wise-transformations/" target="_blank" rel="noopener">distill.pub/2018/feature-wise-transformations</a></p>
<p style="font-size:0.8rem; color:var(--text-muted)">Survey showing conditional batch/instance normalization as special cases of FiLM. Key theoretical reference.</p>
</div>

<div class="ref-card">
<p><span class="ref-num">[9]</span> Internal benchmark synthesis (2026). <em>IKEA ASM external-results summary</em> from <code>deepsearch2/unified.md</code> and <code>deepresearch.md</code> used in this project workspace.</p>
<p style="font-size:0.8rem; color:var(--text-muted)">Used only as contextual range for external systems (for example, 72–73% with temporal/context-heavy setups); exact apples-to-apples comparison requires matched protocol and released code.</p>
</div>

<div class="ref-card">
<p><span class="ref-num">[10]</span> Gu, J. et al. (2025). <em>Motion-guided Modulation Network for Skeleton-based Micro-Action Recognition (MMN).</em> <span class="ref-venue">ACM MM 2025.</span> <a href="https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.21977" target="_blank" rel="noopener">arxiv.org/abs/2507.21977</a></p>
<p style="font-size:0.8rem; color:var(--text-muted)">Closest related work — FiLM-style modulation in skeleton feature space for micro-actions. Differs from ours: modulates skeleton features (not CNN features).</p>
</div>

<div class="ref-card">
<p><span class="ref-num">[11]</span> He, K., Zhang, X., Ren, S., &amp; Sun, J. (2015). <em>Delving Deep into Rectifiers: Surpassing Human-Level Performance on ImageNet Classification.</em> <span class="ref-venue">ICCV 2015.</span> <a href="https://arxiv.org/abs/1502.01852" target="_blank" rel="noopener">arxiv.org/abs/1502.01852</a></p>
<p style="font-size:0.8rem; color:var(--text-muted)">Kaiming initialization for ReLU networks. Used for initializing FiLM MLP layers.</p>
</div>

<div class="ref-card">
<p><span class="ref-num">[12]</span> Dosovitskiy, A., Beyer, L., Kolesnikov, A., Weissenborn, D., Zhai, X., Unterthiner, T., ... &amp; Houlsby, N. (2021). <em>An Image is Worth 16x16 Words: Transformers for Image Recognition at Scale.</em> <span class="ref-venue">ICLR 2021.</span> <a href="https://arxiv.org/abs/2010.11929" target="_blank" rel="noopener">arxiv.org/abs/2010.11929</a></p>
<p style="font-size:0.8rem; color:var(--text-muted)">Vision Transformer (ViT). Practical takeaway for this project: from-scratch ViTs are data-hungry; strong pretraining can reduce that gap, but ResNet-50 remains a robust baseline in limited-supervision assembly settings.</p>
</div>

</section>
